[[file:~/.claude/plans/twinkling-meandering-graham.md][Plan]]

At any given level of the task tree — top-level headings, and
independently among each heading's own direct children — at most one
heading should ever be =NEXT= at a time, mirroring GTD's single "next
action" idea: two new =org-trigger-hook= functions (alongside the
existing pair from "Enforce the transition rules", ID
39b08ecd-6350-48a2-b169-98535b626ac7) demote a superseded =NEXT=
sibling back to =TODO=, and auto-promote a sole surviving =TODO= in a
sibling group of two or more. See the plan for the full design,
including the resolved double-NEXT race and the two decisions made
during planning: a lone heading with no siblings is never
auto-promoted, and automatic transitions log identically to a manual
one plus an explanatory note.

*Verified, not just implemented:* =claude-code-ide-org--map-siblings=
(new shared helper) walks a heading's same-level siblings via org's own
=org-get-next-sibling=/=org-get-previous-sibling=, which stop at the
enclosing parent's boundary on their own — no special-casing needed
for top-level headings. Two new =org-trigger-hook= functions,
=claude-code-ide-org--trigger-demote-conflicting-next= and
=claude-code-ide-org--trigger-auto-promote-sole-todo=, registered
alongside the existing pair. Both deliberately carry no re-entrancy
boolean guard (unlike =claude-code-ide-org--auto-clock-in-active=) —
correctness is structural instead: each only re-derives sibling state
fresh from the live buffer, never from the hook's =change-plist=, which
is exactly what prevents the double-=NEXT= race (naively re-promoting a
heading the demote function just demoted). 11 new regression tests
cover top-level and direct-children demotion, cross-subtree isolation,
promotion on a non-NEXT transition (e.g. a sibling going =DONE=),
leaving a non-=TODO= or a two-=TODO= survivor group alone, the
race case itself, a pre-existing-invalid-state collapse, enforcement
via a bare =org-todo= call (not just the wrapper), and the
group-of-one exclusion (confirmed against the user's explicit call:
auto-promotion requires >= 2 siblings). One pre-existing test
(=...blocker-hook-only-blocks-own-heading=) was swept and fixed — it
incidentally created a 2-heading TODO-survivor group the new promote
hook would otherwise have mutated, unrelated to what that test
actually checks; isolated via a local =let=-shadow of
=org-trigger-hook=, matching the existing isolation pattern already
used by =...set-todo-reports-blocked-transition=. =bin/test= passes
106/106. Also verified live against the running Doom Emacs (reload,
then two separate scratch-file checks): demoting a superseded =NEXT=
sibling correctly writes an "Auto-demoted" =:LOGBOOK:= note and leaves
the new =NEXT= alone; reducing a 2-=TODO= group to one survivor via a
=DONE= transition (not a =NEXT= transition) correctly auto-promotes
the survivor with an "Auto-promoted" note; neither case triggered an
interactive note prompt.


*Refined 2026-08-11: the auto-demote note names its sibling by
=[[id:...]]= link, not by quoted title.* Requested after asking whether a
demotion reason was captured at all — it already was, and had been since
this heading shipped, but it named the superseding sibling by title.

Titles here are not stable. They get revised as scope clarifies —
=c954f650= was retitled the same day — and instability is precisely why
every tool in this module addresses headings by =:ID:=. A quoted title
inside a note that exists to explain *why* something was demoted is the
last place a name should be allowed to go quietly wrong. The link is also
navigable, which a quoted title is not.

Falls back to the quoted title when the superseding sibling has no
=:ID:=: a slightly stale name beats a dangling =[[id:nil]]=, and beats
suppressing the note entirely.

*Verified:* =bin/test= 164/164. The top-level demotion test now asserts
the id form specifically and asserts the bare-title form is *absent*,
since a title-only match would have passed against the old format too;
confirmed to fail against the pre-change =config.el= by stashing it. A
second test pins the no-=:ID:= fallback — and its docstring records that
it passes against the old implementation as well, so nobody mistakes it
for evidence of this change.

*Not yet observed in the wild.* No real demotion has occurred: every
=NEXT= promotion in these files so far has happened with no sibling
holding =NEXT=. The two "Auto-demoted" strings in TODO.org are prose in
outcome notes, not logged transitions.
** DONE Adopt org's native !/@ state-change logging, guarded by org-inhibit-logging :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       04d0e7d5-ab6b-4972-925d-d517484c7595
:CREATED:  [2026-08-04 Tue 13:10]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-04 Tue 13:10]--[2026-08-04 Tue 13:20] =>  0:10
:END:
Follow-up to the sibling heading above: add =!=/=@= markers to the
=#+TODO:= line (=WAIT(w@/!)=, =CANCELLED(c@)=, =!= elsewhere) so org's
own native state-change logging captures transition history for free.
Since =WAIT=/=CANCELLED= are almost always set by Claude through
=org_set_todo= (a non-interactive path) rather than a live hand-edit,
=@='s interactive note-prompt would risk hanging/dropping state on that
path. Planned fix: let-bind =org-inhibit-logging= around the wrapper's
own =org-todo= call specifically for those two states, so =@= only ever
fires for a genuine hand-edit made directly in the Emacs buffer — the
one case where a human is actually present to answer it. The two
single-NEXT-per-level trigger hooks get the same guard around their
own nested =org-todo= calls (defensive — neither =TODO= nor =NEXT= is
=@=-flagged today, but this keeps them immune regardless of future
marker changes), and their existing hand-written notes get reformatted
to match org's own native =state= log-line template
(=State %-12s from %-12s %t=) instead of a separate generic "Note
taken on" line with a redundant second timestamp.

*Verified, not just implemented — and the plan changed twice under
live testing:*
1. First surprise: confirmed live that even a plain =!= marker (not
   just =@=) defers its log-line insertion through the same
   =org-add-log-note=/=post-command-hook= machinery, and that deferred
   call *blocks indefinitely* when triggered non-interactively via
   =emacsclient -e= — reproduced directly (had to =kill -9= the stuck
   process), confirmed =org-inhibit-logging= resolves it. This
   invalidated the planned "selective, =@=-states-only" guard:
   =claude-code-ide-org-set-todo= now binds =org-inhibit-logging=
   unconditionally, for every =STATE=, not just =WAIT=/=CANCELLED=.
2. Second surprise, found chasing an unrelated live failure: with the
   logging fix in place, driving a demote cascade through the wrapper
   itself (not bare =org-todo=, which is all the regression tests used)
   made =org_set_todo= report a false =Error= for a transition that had
   actually succeeded. Instrumented trace showed a marker captured
   before the nested =org-todo= call held the same raw position
   afterward while the *text* at that position had changed — most
   likely =ws-butler-mode= (a whitespace-cleanup minor mode in the
   user's personal Doom config, absent from the =emacs --batch -Q= ERT
   environment) replacing a wider region than a plain insert. Fix: the
   wrapper's post-transition check now re-resolves the heading fresh by
   =:ID:= (=org-id-find=) after =org-todo= returns, exactly like every
   regression test already does, rather than trusting a saved marker or
   "current point" — immune to the drift regardless of its root cause.
2 new regression tests added directly from these findings
(=...suppresses-native-logging-for-every-state=,
=...reports-success-when-hook-cascade-moves-point=), plus one
confirming no =*Org Note*= buffer ever appears for =WAIT=/=CANCELLED=
through the wrapper. =bin/test= passes 109/109. Live-verified end to
end against the running Doom Emacs (reload, then real scratch-file
calls through =claude-code-ide-org-set-todo= itself, not bare
=org-todo=): a two-sibling demote cascade (=NEXT= then =NEXT= again)
now reports success correctly for both calls, with the combined
=State ... \\ Auto-demoted: ...= note format present; =WAIT= then
=CANCELLED= on the same heading both succeed with no =*Org Note*=
buffer ever created.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/quirky-petting-lobster.md][Plan]]

Raised in conversation 2026-08-04, following the finding that Claude
Code has no dedicated Plan Mode enter/exit hook — only =PreToolUse=/
=PostToolUse= matched on the =ExitPlanMode= tool call, and nothing
fires on *entering* plan mode since that transition isn't a tool call
at all.

=PLANNING= slots between =NEXT= and =DOING=. Entry is a model
judgment call made *before* calling =EnterPlanMode= (Plan Mode itself
forbids non-readonly tool calls, so =org_set_todo= cannot run once
inside it — confirmed empirically during planning). Exit is
automatic: a =PostToolUse= hook matched on =ExitPlanMode= promotes
=PLANNING= → =DOING= in place, reusing the same continuous clock
interval rather than closing and reopening it. A "plan and implement"
prompt still produces both transitions at their correct, separate
times, never a premature jump to =DOING=.

Plan approved 2026-08-04, resolving the three original open
questions: =PLANNING= opens a clock exactly like =DOING= (no
=:LOGBOOK:= distinction between planning and implementation time);
the existing =claude-code-ide-org--trigger-auto-clock-in= trigger
hook gets a one-line =member= check extended to =PLANNING=, no new
hook needed; and no session→heading mapping is required at all —
because only one clock can run globally and =PLANNING= is only ever
set by the model on its own heading, "promote whichever heading
currently owns the clock marker, iff its state is exactly PLANNING"
is sufficient, mirroring
=claude-code-ide-org--blocker-clock-running-p='s existing
clock-marker-identity pattern.

One new gap surfaced *during* planning itself, not anticipated up
front: there's no reliable plan-approval/rejection signal available
to gate the promotion on. Confirmed empirically, not just assumed —
attempting to instrument a temporary capture hook to inspect a live
=ExitPlanMode= payload is itself a non-readonly action, which
re-triggers Plan Mode before the capture can run, which then blocks
the very instrumentation needed to capture it: circular by
construction. Accepted as a documented gap (same register as the
concurrent-session clock entry's "self-corrects" tolerance above) —
a stray promotion after a rejected plan is low-cost and self-corrects
the next time the heading's real state is set explicitly. See the
plan link for the full design and file-by-file implementation
breakdown.

*Verified, not just implemented:* the implementing session (2026-08-04)
covered =bin/test= regression coverage and manual reload/scratch-file
checks of the mechanics, but explicitly could not exercise the real
=ExitPlanMode= tool-call path end to end — =.claude/settings.json='s
hook config only loads at session start, and the new =PostToolUse=
entries (matcher =mcp__emacs-tools__org_set_todo=, matcher
=ExitPlanMode=) postdated that session's own hook config load. A
follow-up session (2026-08-05) closed that gap live, against a
disposable scratch heading in TODO.org, no code changes: =TODO= →
=NEXT= → =PLANNING= via =org_set_todo=, confirmed
=claude-code-ide-org--planning-owner-session-id= was set to the
calling session's =session_id= (=bin/hooks/posttooluse-record-planning-owner=
firing on the =org_set_todo= =PostToolUse= event); then a real
=EnterPlanMode=/=ExitPlanMode= round trip (trivial one-line plan,
approved) drove the heading to =DOING= with exactly one =CLOCK:= line
(no duplicate interval), the expected "Auto-promoted: ExitPlanMode
fired on the owning session." =:LOGBOOK:= note, and
=claude-code-ide-org--planning-owner-session-id= back to =nil= —
all with zero manual =emacsclient= intervention beyond read-only
inspection to confirm each step. Scratch heading clocked out,
=CANCELLED=, and deleted afterward. Not exercised here (still exactly
the accepted gaps from planning, not new findings): the rejected-plan
promotion gap described above ("One new gap surfaced during planning
itself...") , and the cross-session owner guard documented separately
in CLAUDE.md's "State transition rules" section (the "Cross-session
guard" paragraph, CLAUDE.md:193-196) rather than in this heading's own
body. Both would require a second concurrent session or a plan
rejection to trigger, neither of which this pass attempted.

Blocked on the sibling heading above (=Add a PLANNING TODO state,
driven by the ExitPlanMode matcher=, ID
b95b9fba-f78e-48fe-8546-988709cce309), recorded via the project's
=:BLOCKER:= convention (native org-depend syntax; see the org skill
for the full documentation) — not currently enforced by this
project's own =org-blocker-hook= function, which only blocks on a
running clock, not on =:BLOCKER:= (see the sibling heading below for
closing that gap). *Update 2026-08-05: the blocker is now satisfied*
— =b95b9fba= is =DONE=, =PLANNING= is fully implemented, so this

Property left in place regardless (a satisfied =:BLOCKER:= is
harmless under org-depend semantics and preserves why this was

Captured 2026-08-04 from conversation, deliberately at "capture the
idea" depth, not fully designed yet:

Whenever the user says to modify some feature (in the org skill's
scope), first check whether the request matches any existing TODO
heading, including =DONE= ones:
- If it matches an existing heading, ask the user for clarification
  (e.g. "this looks like it overlaps with <heading> — same task, or
  something new?") rather than silently creating a duplicate or
  silently assuming it's the same thing.
- If it doesn't match anything, create a new heading, capture it (via
  =org_capture=), set it to =PLANNING=, ask the user any necessary
  clarifying questions, and then offer to plan it properly (i.e. enter
  Plan Mode). If the user declines planning, revert the heading back
  to =TODO= for later planning rather than leaving it stuck in
  =PLANNING=.

Separately, also check whether the proposed change interacts with any
*open* (non-DONE, non-CANCELLED) task via a depends-on relationship in
either direction — i.e. either the new change depends on unfinished
work, or some existing open task depends on the thing being changed —
and surface that to the user.

Open questions deferred to actual planning, not resolved here: how
"matches an existing TODO" is actually determined (semantic search via
=org_query=? something else?), whether discovering an existing
=:BLOCKER:= depends-on link (this heading's own, above) is sufficient
or whether the check also needs to search prose for informal
cross-references that predate this convention, and exactly when in the
org skill's flow this check should fire.

** DONE Implement the ten pending MCP-tool tasks in parallel worktrees :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       3d862c6c-6d03-4fe9-9923-067966ef943a
:CREATED:  [2026-08-12 Wed 12:33]
:END:

[[file:~/.claude/plans/send-each-mcp-feature-zesty-bunny.md][Plan]]

*This heading is retrospective, and deliberately not backdated.* The
work ran on 2026-07-28/29; the heading was written 2026-08-12 while
resolving [[id:cce72644-7d5e-4def-a165-0a3b815f482b][cce72644]], because
the plan file had no owner and CLAUDE.md's archive rule is that a plan is
kept iff some heading links it. =:CREATED:= is the moment this heading
was created, per the convention, not the moment the work started — the
real dates are in the ten task headings and in the plan file itself.

*What it was.* Orchestration, not design: ten tasks in TODO.org's then
"New MCP tools" section and its MCP-adjacent siblings already carried
fully worked-out checklists, and the plan covered getting all ten built
in isolated worktrees and integrated back one at a time without losing
work or breaking =bin/test=. Written 2026-07-28 21:23; the first task
was archived 2026-07-29 01:57.

*Outcome: all ten shipped, and all ten are archived in DONE.org* —
=org_capture=, =org_clock_report=, =org_refile=,
=org_sort_children=/=org_move_sibling=, the
=org-trigger-hook=/=org-blocker-hook= pair and its config-test.el
extension, the tool-call audit log, surface-current-clock-state, the
PreToolUse transition guard, clock-change notifications, and the
SessionStart context hook. Only five carried an =:ID:= at plan time; the
other five were =(none yet)= in the plan's own table and were created as
the work reached them.

*No clock or =:LOGBOOK:= here, on purpose.* Every one of those ten
headings carries its own intervals, and org's clocktable sums a subtree
by plain addition — so time recorded on an umbrella is *additive* with
the children's rather than a roll-up of it. Same reasoning as
[[id:ab75d6d2-0e59-405d-92c6-2c67488db133][ab75d6d2]]. These ten are not
even children of this heading, so any interval here would be pure
double-count.

*Superseded in one respect, worth knowing before reusing it.* The plan's
load-bearing constraint — clock and state changes are coordinator-only,
one task at a time, because org's clock is a single global resource that
subagents in isolated worktrees cannot safely touch — is exactly the
problem [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]]'s event
queue replaced. The worktree orchestration still stands; the clocking
discipline around it does not.

** DONE Org skill: parallelize background planning across the NEXT/TODO backlog, without touching the shared clock :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       d8444673-b047-4755-b62c-d03df738f16a
:CREATED:  [2026-08-05 Wed 16:12]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
:END:

[[file:~/.claude/plans/warm-marinating-puddle.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-05 from conversation: how to kick off planning for
every open NEXT/TODO heading in parallel, while getting the resulting
=:SESSIONS:= bookkeeping right, given the constraint (confirmed live,
repeatedly, this same day) that org's clock is a single global
resource shared across every session touching this file.

*Core rule, structural not just cautious*: background planning agents
never transition any heading's TODO keyword and never call
=org_clock_in=/=org_set_todo=-to-=PLANNING=. Both
=claude-code-ide-org--planning-owner-session-id= and
=claude-code-ide-org--clock-owner-session-id= are single global
variables, and =claude-code-ide-org--promote-planning-to-doing=
promotes "whichever heading currently owns the running clock" — so N
simultaneous =PLANNING= owners is structurally impossible, not merely
awkward. There is exactly one clock. Background agents do read-only
research and write only their own plan file
(=~/.claude/plans/<slug>.md=); nothing about that touches the shared
clock, so it is the only part of this safe to genuinely parallelize.

*Write-back* (applying results to =TODO.org= — the Plan link plus a
session-bracketing =:SESSIONS:= note per heading):
- Serialize it: one heading at a time, only after each agent reports
  done.
- Route every write through =emacsclient=/elisp, not a raw external
  file write, even for headings with no clock currently running on
  them — unconditional consistency, not a per-call "is it safe right
  now" check. Directly motivated by
  [[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][Direct file writes to a live org buffer can desync org-clock-marker]].
- Use =claude-code-ide-org--log-session-event= for the synthetic
  =:SESSIONS:= entries rather than hand-writing text — keeps the
  format forward-compatible with
  [[id:2b0988ed-eb92-4fa7-904a-cb2d10941bfd][Reorder :SESSIONS: entries to match :LOGBOOK: ordering]]'s pending
  flip, and guarantees correct drawer-append semantics.
- Re-resolve each heading fresh by =:ID:= at write time
  (=claude-code-ide-org--at-id=, the pattern every existing tool
  already uses) rather than trusting any position cached from when
  the agent was launched — other sessions touch this file constantly,
  demonstrated repeatedly today, including while this very design was
  being written up.
- Tag the synthetic =:SESSIONS:= entry with a distinct synthetic
  identifier, never this orchestrating session's own real
  =session_id= — misattributing unattended background research time
  as this session's own interactive work would be exactly the kind of
  noise
  [[id:782cda6c-111c-454e-93eb-b2b27012078b][Audit for clock/session bookkeeping noise before committing during a clocking-suppressed session]]
  is worried about.
- No =:LOGBOOK:=/CLOCK entries at all, by design — the single-clock
  model cannot represent true parallelism honestly, so don't try.
- Don't auto-commit the resulting batch. Leave it for explicit
  review — finalizing an auto-commit policy here would mean guessing
  at exactly the still-open question in
  [[id:3cb3f955-f10a-47cd-84ab-e629d73ea59d][Org skill: commit a newly-captured TODO immediately, when safe]]
  and [[id:782cda6c-111c-454e-93eb-b2b27012078b][the sibling heading above]].

Motivated concretely, not hypothetically: while this design was being
reviewed in the same conversation, this session's own ordinary
=UserPromptSubmit= resume hook reopened an unrelated heading's clock
*twice* and briefly produced a malformed, timestamp-less =CLOCK:=
line — see
[[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][Direct file writes to a live org buffer can desync org-clock-marker]]'s
"Second, distinct incident" note. That happened from ordinary
incidental multi-session activity, not deliberate parallel load —
strong first-party evidence for why deliberately launching several
background agents at once needs the constraints above, not weaker
ones.

Open questions left for actual planning: exact format/label for the
synthetic =:SESSIONS:= identifier; how many agents to launch per
batch and whether that needs a cap; whether "no clock entries at all"
should extend to *every* unattended/background invocation of this
skill or just this specific batch-planning use case.

*Resolved during planning (2026-08-05):* synthetic identifier is
=<orchestrating-session-id>-bg<N>= (derived from, never equal to, the
real session id); batch size capped at 3; "no clock entries" scoped
to this specific background-planning write-back path only, not
generalized to every background invocation.

*Implementation notes:* added
=claude-code-ide-org-log-background-plan= (id, plan-file, session-id)
plus its =claude-code-ide-org--insert-plan-link= helper, exposed as
the new MCP tool =org_log_background_plan=. Reuses
=claude-code-ide-org--at-id= and =claude-code-ide-org--log-session-event=
exactly as specified above; inserts the Plan link idempotently
(=org-end-of-meta-data t= to land after the property drawer and any
=:SESSIONS:=/=:LOGBOOK:= drawers) and never calls =org-todo=,
=org-clock-in=, or =org-clock-out=. Documented the orchestration
workflow (query → batch of ≤3 background agents → serialized
write-back) as a new section in the org skill
(=.claude/skills/org/SKILL.md=), and added the new tool to CLAUDE.md's
MCP tools table.

*Verified, not just implemented:* four new ERT tests
(=config-test.el=) cover link+:SESSIONS: insertion, idempotency
across repeated calls with different plan-file paths, that TODO
state/clock/owner-vars are untouched, and fresh :ID: re-resolution
under buffer mutation between calls — =bin/test= passes 122/122
(118 pre-existing + 4 new). Also live-reloaded =config.el= into the
running Doom Emacs and ran a manual smoke test against a scratch
heading via =emacsclient=: confirmed the Plan link and a
"Background-planned" =:SESSIONS:= entry appear, no =:LOGBOOK:= entry
or TODO-state change occurs, and a second call with a different
plan-file path does not duplicate the Plan link.

** MAYBE Make org-blocker-hook actually consult :BLOCKER: :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       087e73e9-bb24-43b6-a972-b7f103d04017
:CREATED:  [2026-08-04 Tue 17:46]

[[file:~/.claude/plans/blocker-hook-enforcement.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-04, surfaced while adding a =:BLOCKER:= property to
the sibling heading above: this project's own
=claude-code-ide-org--blocker-clock-running-p= (the one function
currently on =org-blocker-hook=) only denies a =DONE= transition while
a clock is running on that same heading — it never reads =:BLOCKER:=
at all, and org-depend itself isn't loaded anywhere in this project
(=packages.el= notes no additional packages). So every existing
=:BLOCKER:= property in =TODO.org=/=DONE.org= (e.g. ID
799b14be-6849-4e22-8a76-4e214d8a25fe's) is purely advisory today —
=org_set_todo= will happily transition a blocked heading to =DONE=
with nothing stopping it, contrary to what the property visually
implies.

Extend =claude-code-ide-org--blocker-clock-running-p= (or add a second
=org-blocker-hook= function alongside it) to also deny a transition to
=DONE= while any ID listed in the heading's own =:BLOCKER:= property
resolves to a heading that isn't itself in a done state (=DONE= or
=CANCELLED=). Should reuse =org-id-find= the same way the existing
clock-marker check and =org_set_todo='s post-transition heading
lookup already do. Whether to also honor the inverse =:TRIGGER:=
property (documented in the org skill alongside =:BLOCKER:=) is an
open question for planning — =org-depend= itself implements both, but
this project may only need one direction enforced.

** MAYBE Org skill: commit a newly-captured TODO immediately, when safe :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       3cb3f955-f10a-47cd-84ab-e629d73ea59d
:CREATED:  [2026-08-04 Tue 17:54]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-06 Thu 12:07] \\
  Auto-promoted: sole remaining TODO in its sibling group.
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/capture-commit-safety.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-04 from conversation: right now, capturing a new org
heading (per CLAUDE.md's "any time a new task is described in
conversation, create an org heading" rule) only edits the working tree
— nothing commits it, so it sits as an uncommitted change alongside
whatever else is in progress until a human notices and commits by
hand. Tell the org skill to commit the capture itself, immediately,
*given that it's safe to do so*.

"Safe" needs pinning down during planning, but at minimum: only the
=.org= file(s) touched by the capture are staged (never a broad
=git add -A=/=git add .=, per this project's own general git-safety
practice), the repo isn't mid-rebase/mid-merge/otherwise in a weird
state, and — the tricky part — the capture commit doesn't get tangled
up with *other* unrelated uncommitted changes already sitting in the
same file from earlier in the session (a commit should be scoped to
the capture, not sweep up unrelated in-progress edits just because
they're in the same file). Whether that means a targeted
=git commit -- TODO.org= (commits the whole file's current diff,
including any unrelated hunks) is actually "safe" in the sense meant
here, or whether something narrower is needed, is exactly the kind of
question actual planning should resolve rather than deciding here.

Should this be prose guidance in the skill (the model decides "is this
one safe" case by case, same register as the existing read-only-buffer
guidance) or something more mechanical? Lean prose, given the "safe"
judgment call looks inherently contextual rather than a fixed
checklist — same reasoning CLAUDE.md already uses elsewhere for calls
that "only the model can make."

*Update 2026-08-05:* substantial overlap with
[[id:782cda6c-111c-454e-93eb-b2b27012078b][Audit for clock/session bookkeeping noise before committing during a clocking-suppressed session]]
— that heading is a concrete, evidence-backed version of the exact
open question above ("doesn't get tangled up with other unrelated
uncommitted changes"). Not merging them here; worth deciding during
planning whether they should be one task, sequenced, or just
cross-referenced as they are now.

Sharper still: proposal 1 below (add a =VERIFY= keyword) was not merely
survived by the refactor, it was *generated* by it. Human-triggered

Promoted =MAYBE= -> =TODO= with no replacement condition. If it stalls
again, the reason should be recorded as a real blocker rather than a

-----

*Scope widened 2026-08-10, and the heading retitled to match.* What
started as "is =PLANNING= pulling its weight" became a review of the
whole progression, after checking the sequence against org+GTD practice
on the web. The original question is a sub-case and is answered in part
below.

*Census of TODO.org the same day, which is the most useful input here:*

: 23 MAYBE   14 TODO   14 DONE   2 NEXT   2 DOING   1 CANCELLED
:  0 PLANNING   0 WAIT

Those 56 are the *keyworded* headings; the file holds 63, the
difference being the seven top-level category headings, which carry no
TODO keyword by convention.

*What the tradition says — both findings cut against having =DOING=:*
- The canonical org+GTD reference (Bernt Hansen, https://doc.norang.ca/org-mode.html)
  runs =TODO(t) NEXT(n) | DONE(d!)= plus =WAITING(w@/!) HOLD(h@/!) |
  CANCELLED(c@)=. No in-progress keyword at all: *clocking* signals what
  is active, and he goes as far as switching tasks *to* =NEXT= on
  clock-in. =NEXT= means "available to work on now."
- GTD proper treats an in-progress state as a smell. A next action is
  "the next physical, visible activity"; anything needing an
  in-progress marker is usually a project wearing an action's clothes,
  and the prescribed fix is decomposition, not another keyword.

*=DOING= survives that critique here, for the reason it usually fails
it.* The standard substitute — let the clock say what is active — is
exactly the affordance
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] removes. CLAUDE.md
already commits to clock accuracy being "only ever as fresh as the last
time a human ran the review pass, not live." A reviewed historical
record cannot answer "what is being worked on right now," so the
keyword has to. Worth recording explicitly, because a future reader
citing Bernt or GTD would have a well-supported argument for deleting
=DOING= and would be wrong *for this system specifically*.

The user's own narrowing helps too: =DOING= means actively working on
implementation and testing — a named phase, not an open-ended
in-progress limbo, which is what GTD actually objects to.

*Proposal 1, the substantive one: add =VERIFY= between =DOING= and
=DONE=.* Headings here repeatedly sit in =DOING= carrying "*Still
owed:* an interactive by-hand pass" — implementation and tests
complete, verification outstanding. Under the definition above that is
not =DOING=, and under this project's "verified, not just implemented"
bar it is certainly not =DONE=. The state is *structural*, not
incidental: verification is human-triggered by hard constraint (Claude
cannot run the interactive apply), so every such task passes through a
mandatory handoff that currently has no name and lives only as prose.
org's own manual uses exactly this slot in its example sequence,
=#+TODO: TODO FEEDBACK VERIFY | DONE CANCELED= (org-manual.org:4210).

The payoff is a real queue: =VERIFY= becomes "what is waiting on *me*
to confirm," which is a next-action list in the GTD sense and does not
exist today in any queryable form.

: #+TODO: TODO NEXT PLANNING DOING VERIFY WAIT MAYBE | DONE CANCELLED

Clock rule if adopted: =DOING= → =VERIFY= closes the clock, since Claude
has stopped; the human's verification time clocks against the same
heading, which the convention recorded at
[[id:a1d63f52-4c07-4b9e-8f31-6d2b90c4e7a5][a1d63f52]] already permits.

*Proposal 2: get =MAYBE= out of the default agenda without changing the
keyword.* At 23 of the file's 56 keyworded headings it is the largest —
a perfectly healthy Someday/Maybe list, except that sitting before the
=|= renders it actionable. GTD is emphatic that Someday/Maybe lives
outside the active system and is reviewed periodically. Bernt's answer
is to keep such states active but filter them from default agenda views;
moving =MAYBE= after the =|= would be semantically wrong, since these
are not finished.

*Proposal 3: narrow =WAIT= to "waiting on a person."* It has never
been used once. GTD separates *Waiting For* (delegated, needs follow-up)
from *blocked* (dependency), and dependencies here are already expressed
structurally via =:BLOCKER:=. The current gloss, "blocked or waiting on
someone," overlaps that — and a keyword nobody reaches for is usually
one whose meaning is unclear rather than one whose situation never
arises.

*Bearing on this heading's original question.* The tradition supports a
planning state on its merits: planning is first-class GTD activity (the
Natural Planning Model), and =PLANNING= is on that basis *better*
grounded than =DOING=. But that answers only half the complaint recorded
above. Whether the *machinery* earns its complexity — the
=ExitPlanMode=-driven auto-promotion, the single global
=--planning-owner-session-id=, the hand-reversions logged above — is a
separate question the tradition says nothing about, and it stays open.
Do not read "GTD likes planning states" as "keep the hook."

Still =MAYBE=, still deferred on the original terms. Nothing above is
adopted; the sequence is unchanged pending a decision.

** CANCELLED org_capture omits the :CREATED: property its own convention requires :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       cdcc065c-628d-4350-b1ba-fcf686e9c4c1
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 07:31]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "CANCELLED"  from "TODO"       [2026-08-11 Tue 18:19] \\
  folded into 47c027d2 as one template edit
:END:

CLAUDE.md is unambiguous: "any newly created org heading gets a
=:CREATED:= property in its property drawer, stamped with an inactive
timestamp at creation time, alongside its =:ID:=. Applies to every
heading creation." The one tool whose entire job is creating headings
does not do it.

Its template, built fresh per call in =claude-code-ide-org-capture=, is
exactly:

: "* TODO %i\n:PROPERTIES:\n:ID:       %s\n:END:\n"

Title, =:ID:=, nothing else. Confirmed 2026-08-11 by grep: the string
=CREATED= appears nowhere in config.el, so nothing adds it afterwards
either. Every =:CREATED:= currently in TODO.org is there because a human
or Claude hand-wrote it while composing the heading in Elisp — which is
also why the gap has gone unnoticed: the path that honours the rule is
the path that bypasses the tool.

*Fix:* add =:CREATED:  %%U= to the template. org-capture's =%U= expands
to an inactive timestamp with date and time, which is precisely the
convention's format. Note the doubled =%%=: the template is assembled
with =format=, which is why the existing title escape is written =%%i=.
Match the drawer's existing alignment (=:CREATED:= followed by two
spaces) so captured headings are indistinguishable from hand-written
ones.

*Test:* the existing capture coverage should additionally assert a
=:CREATED:= that parses as an inactive timestamp — not merely that the
string is present, since a template escape that silently fails to expand
would leave a literal =%U= and still match a naive check.

Worth reading with [[id:7fa68d5c-b6c0-43df-85c6-764b1d56511f][7fa68d5c]], which
records the larger reason this matters less than it looks: almost
nothing in this project is captured through this tool.

*Folded into
[[id:47c027d2-7b5a-4de0-baf4-f992f3b814bd][47c027d2]], 2026-08-11.*
=CANCELLED= as a *separate* task, not abandoned: the missing =:CREATED:=
is real and still gets fixed, but it turned out to be one of three
defects in the same four-line template string, alongside the hardcoded
=TODO= keyword and the file-only capture target. Treating them
separately meant editing that string three times. This heading stays in
place rather than being deleted because five other headings link to it;
the analysis above is still the reference for *why* =:CREATED:= is owed
and how to verify it, and =47c027d2= carries the work.

** TODO Fall back to the queue when org_capture cannot reach Emacs :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       7fa68d5c-b6c0-43df-85c6-764b1d56511f
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 07:31]
:END:

=org_capture= requires a live Emacs. When the server is unreachable the
call fails outright and the task exists only in the conversation, which
ends. Not hypothetical: on 2026-08-10 the Emacs server was down for the
first ten minutes or so of a session (=emacsclient= reporting "can't
find socket"), and any capture attempted then would simply have been
lost.

This is the one creation path that cannot use the property the event
queue exists for — durable, cheap, no Emacs required. Everything else a
session records survives an Emacs outage; the act of writing down a new
task does not.

*Proposal:* attempt the live capture; on failure, append a =capture=
event to the queue, and let apply create the heading.

*Three things this raises, none of them settled:*
- *It is the queue's first outbox use.* Every existing kind is a
  proposal about a heading that already exists; a =capture= event would
  bring one into being. That is a genuinely different contract, and the
  review buffer would need to render it as such.
- *The :ID: must be minted by the session, not at apply time.* An ID
  assigned on application is useless to the session that queued it — it
  could not link to the heading, block on it, or clock against it, which
  is exactly what =claude-code-ide-org-capture= returns its ID for
  today. Minting without Emacs is fine (=org-id-new= is a UUID), but it
  means an ID can be referenced before the heading it names exists.
- *Reconciliation.* If the live capture succeeded and only its reply was
  lost, applying the queued event would duplicate the heading. A
  session-minted ID gives apply a cheap idempotency key: if that :ID:
  already resolves, the event is already realised.

*The larger caveat, which bears on this and on
[[id:cdcc065c-628d-4350-b1ba-fcf686e9c4c1][cdcc065c]] both.* This tool creates a bare
=* TODO <title>= at top level in a single target file. It cannot place a
heading under a named parent, nor carry tags, a body, or any property
beyond the =:ID:= it generates. Consequently almost every heading in
this project is written by direct Elisp instead — four were created that
way on 2026-08-10 alone, none through the tool. So instrumenting or
repairing =org_capture= improves a path that is rarely taken, and the
prior question is whether the tool should become capable enough to be
worth using. Decide that first; it may reorder or absorb both headings.

*Considered and set aside:* queueing a =capture= event on *every*
capture, as an audit record carrying =session_id=/=agent_id= — which is
the only place session attribution exists, and would answer "which
session created this heading?" structurally rather than from prose in
the body. Rejected for now on the caveat above: it would record almost
nothing real. Revisit if the tool becomes the normal creation path.


*Also the timeline origin:* recording creation as a =:LOGBOOK:= entry
([[id:d9132c57-9dcf-47e9-ba74-7f5a1ced3ae5][d9132c57]]) can ride this same event rather
than needing a mechanism of its own — apply would write the creation
entry exactly as it writes a state transition.

** DONE Add org_outline, a compact structural index of an org file :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       9f3c35c7-0229-4cd6-908b-779267ec9b97
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 16:58]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 20:11]--[2026-08-11 Tue 21:30] =>  1:19
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-11 Tue 18:26] \\
  org_outline shipped, 157/157 green
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-11 Tue 18:20] \\
  implement org_outline index tool
- <2026-08-11 Tue 20:11>--<2026-08-11 Tue 21:30>
:END:

A read-only MCP tool returning one line per heading — level, TODO
keyword, title, =:ID:=, tags, and whether a =:BLOCKER:= is set — as an
index for answering "what already exists here" and "where does this
belong" without reading the file.

*Measured on TODO.org, 2026-08-11:* 280,079 bytes and 82 headings (7 at
level 1, 45 at level 2, 30 at level 3; 70 carry an =:ID:=) reduce to
7,384 bytes of index, or 5,394 with =DONE=/=CANCELLED= filtered out. A
38x reduction, and the prototype that produced it is most of the
implementation.

: Skill logic
:   DONE Enforce a single NEXT action per level of the task tree  {05da96ad}  :code:
:   MAYBE Make org-blocker-hook actually consult :BLOCKER:  {087e73e9}  :code:
:   TODO Fall back to the queue when org_capture cannot reach Emacs  {7fa68d5c}  :code:

*The motivating case is not placement.* It is that three separate times
on 2026-08-11 a session re-derived something this file already knew —
[[id:cdcc065c-628d-4350-b1ba-fcf686e9c4c1][cdcc065c]],
[[id:5e2a1c04-7b3f-4d21-9c88-2f6e0a91b7d3][5e2a1c04]] and
[[id:d9132c57-9dcf-47e9-ba74-7f5a1ced3ae5][d9132c57]]'s content were each
worked out from scratch and presented as new findings. The cause was not
carelessness in any single instance: checking meant reading 280 KB to
answer a one-line question, which is not viable mid-task, so it kept not
happening. A 7 KB index makes "check what is already known" affordable
enough to become routine, which is the only way that discipline actually
holds.

*Design.* Parameters: =scope= (a file, or a subtree =:ID:= to index just
that branch), =max_depth=, and =active_only= to drop finished states.
Implementation is =org-map-entries= over
=claude-code-ide-org--tracked-files=, the same file list =org_query= and
the recovery check already use. No new dependencies.

*Complementary to =org_query=, not overlapping.* =org_query= answers
"which headings match this predicate" and returns a flat list;
=org_outline= answers "what is the shape of this file and what is in it"
and preserves hierarchy. Neither subsumes the other — the flat list
cannot tell you where a heading sits, and the tree cannot filter by
arbitrary predicate.

*Emit full =:ID:=s, not the 8-character prefixes the prototype shows.*
The prototype truncates for readability, but every other tool in this
project takes a full =:ID:= argument, so a truncated index would force a
second lookup on every use and quietly defeat the point. The cost is
about 2 KB more on this file, which is nothing against 280 KB.

*Landmines to clear before implementing:*
- =org-map-entries= with an explicit file list visits those files; make
  sure it does not leave buffers modified or move point in a buffer the
  user has open. This is a read-only tool and must behave like one.
- Output size is unbounded in principle. This file is small, but the
  tool should not be able to return something enormous — =max_depth= and
  =active_only= are the controls, and a documented cap is worth
  considering.
- Heading titles can contain characters that make the output ambiguous
  to skim. The prototype already showed org escaping =!/@= as =!\\/@= in
  refile paths; check what =org-get-heading= returns versus what the
  refile-target machinery returns, and pick one.

*Related: [[id:47c027d2-7b5a-4de0-baf4-f992f3b814bd][47c027d2]] is
blocked on this*, because correct placement at capture time is only
cheap once the index exists.

*Also worth knowing, from the same investigation:*
=org-refile-get-targets= already returns 168 addressable destinations for
this file as =FILE/Outline/Path= strings, *including the level-1
categories* — which carry no =:ID:= by convention and so cannot be
addressed any other way. =org-refile-targets= is already
=((nil :maxlevel . 3) (org-agenda-files :maxlevel . 3))=, and level 3 is
this file's full depth. That is an existing placement vocabulary, used by
=org_refile= today, and it is what =47c027d2='s target parameter should
accept rather than inventing a category concept.

*Implemented 2026-08-11.* =claude-code-ide-org-outline=, registered as
=org_outline=, with =scope= (an =:ID:= for one subtree, a file name for
one file, or omitted for every tracked file), =max_depth=, and
=active_only=. All three arrive as strings and are parsed leniently:
unusable input falls back to the permissive default rather than erroring,
since an over-large index is recoverable and a failed call is not.

*Shipped sizes, which differ from the estimate above.* Full =:ID:=s cost
about 2 KB more than the truncated prototype: 9,451 bytes for the whole
file, 6,900 with =active_only=. Still a 30x reduction against 280 KB, and
worth it — a truncated index would force a second lookup on every use.

*All three landmines cleared before writing code.* =org-map-entries= over
an explicit file list neither moves point nor marks the buffer modified
(measured against a live open TODO.org: point 124975 before and after).
Output size is bounded by the two parameters. And =org-get-heading t t t
t= returns titles unescaped, unlike =org-refile-get-targets=, which
renders =!/@= as =!\\/@= — fine for a completion table, wrong for
something meant to be read.

*A fourth landmine surfaced only in live use, and is the useful part of
this entry.* The first live call came back as a =#("..." 0 11 (...))=
literal: every accessor here reads from the buffer and carries its text
properties, and =concat= propagates them, so the index would have reached
the MCP layer propertized. Three things went wrong in sequence and each
is worth keeping:

1. *The unit tests could not see it.* Under =emacs --batch -Q= no
   font-lock runs, so a scratch org file yields clean strings and the
   assertion passed whether or not the code stripped anything. An inert
   test that looks like a passing one — the third instance in one day of
   a fixture differing from production in exactly the dimension under
   test, after
   [[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]'s =#+TODO:=
   markers and
   [[id:5e2a1c04-7b3f-4d21-9c88-2f6e0a91b7d3][5e2a1c04]]'s content-block
   payloads. The fix was to propertize the fixture by hand.
2. *The obvious fix was wrong.* Stripping =org-get-heading= alone left
   the result propertized anyway, because =org-get-todo-state=,
   =org-get-tags= and =org-entry-get= all do it too. Caught only because
   the corrected test then failed against the supposedly-fixed code.
3. *The strip belongs at assembly*, over the whole line, not per
   component — one call that cannot be defeated by adding a fourth
   accessor later.

*Verified:* =bin/test= 157/157 (145 pre-existing plus 12 new), the
plain-string test confirmed to fail with the strip removed and pass with
it, and a live call against the real TODO.org returning a plain string.

*Follow-up fix the same evening, found by using the tool.* The first
=[blocked]= marker keyed on the *presence* of a =:BLOCKER:= property.
org-depend treats that property as a durable declaration of dependency,
not a flag it clears when the blocker finishes — so every heading that
ever had one read as blocked permanently. Measured on TODO.org at the
moment it was noticed: *all 10 markers were false*, because all four
referenced headings were =DONE=. Recorded here rather than as a new
heading, since it was found and fixed within the hour and belongs to
this tool's story.

Now resolved rather than asserted: the =:BLOCKER:= value is scanned for
ids, each is looked up, and the marker distinguishes three cases —
=[blocked]= when a named blocker is unfinished, =[blocked?]= when a
dependency exists but cannot be resolved (a dangling id, or a form like
=previous-sibling= that names none), and nothing when all are finished.
Unresolvable is deliberately not collapsed into satisfied; collapsing
the two is how the first version went wrong in the other direction.

Two incidental findings from the survey. TODO.org carries *one*
=:BLOCKER:= written as a bare id with no =ids(...)= wrapper, which
org-depend itself would not honour — the reader handles it anyway,
because reporting that heading as dependency-free would be worse than
reading a malformed value generously. And finishedness is tested against
this file's own =--outline-finished-keywords= rather than
=org-done-keywords=, which is buffer-local and would be nil in the wrong
buffer, quietly reporting every blocker as still open.

*Verified:* =bin/test= 161/161. Three of the five new tests confirmed to
fail against the presence-only marker; the other two pass either way,
because the old code marked *everything* blocked and so matched the
positive cases by accident — kept as pins, not counted as evidence.
Live, the real TODO.org went from 10 =[blocked]= markers to 0.

** DONE Rework org_capture: bare heading, :CREATED:, and a placement target :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       47c027d2-7b5a-4de0-baf4-f992f3b814bd
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 15:52]
:BLOCKER:  ids(9f3c35c7-0229-4cd6-908b-779267ec9b97)
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-11 Tue 21:03] \\
  template and target shipped; defcustom left to the user
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-11 Tue 20:59] \\
  rework org_capture template and target
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-11 Tue 20:55] \\
  blocker satisfied, nominated as next under Skill logic
:END:

*Consolidated 2026-08-11, and the heading retitled to match.* This was
"capture bare headings; apply the keyword at ingestion." Two other
headings turned out to describe edits to the same four lines of the same
function, so they are folded in here:
[[id:cdcc065c-628d-4350-b1ba-fcf686e9c4c1][cdcc065c]] (=org_capture= omits
=:CREATED:=), now =CANCELLED= as a separate task, and the unset
=claude-code-ide-org-capture-file=, whose heading is deleted since
nothing linked to it. The whole of =org_capture= is one template string:

: (format "* TODO %%i\n:PROPERTIES:\n:ID:       %s\n:END:\n" new-id)

Three defects, one edit — treating them as three tasks meant touching
that string three times.

=org_capture= currently writes =* TODO %i= — the keyword is hardcoded in
the template, not a parameter. That asserts a state live while every
other state assertion is queued, which is the cutover's own principle
applied inconsistently.

*Proposed shape (user's, 2026-08-11):* capture writes a bare heading —
=:ID:=, =:CREATED:=, no keyword. Ingestion applies the keyword through
=org-todo= inside the interactive apply, which makes it a genuine
transition and lets org write the creation entry natively.

This is the more conservative split than the "creation *is* a =capture=
event" sketch in
[[id:d9132c57-9dcf-47e9-ba74-7f5a1ced3ae5][d9132c57]]: the heading still
lands immediately, so its =:ID:= is usable and it can be linked and
refiled at once; only the *keyword* defers.

*Verified the mechanism, 2026-08-11*, against the real =#+TODO:= header:
a bare heading taken to =NEXT= or =DOING= produces a native
=- State "NEXT" from [ts]= entry. Taken to =TODO= it produced nothing,
because =TODO= was the one keyword carrying no =!= marker. That was a
hard prerequisite and is now resolved by
[[id:90a5a0cf-a81d-45dc-a453-0bfe61e3ef93][90a5a0cf]]; the remaining
=:BLOCKER:= is the index tool, for placement rather than logging.

It also makes =d9132c57='s duplicate-=:CREATED:=-into-=:LOGBOOK:= vs.
synthesise-at-read-time question moot for anything captured after the
change: org writes the entry, so neither is needed. Backfill is still
owed for the 56 existing headings.

*Accepted cost, confirmed with the user:* between capture and ingestion
the heading matches no =todo:= query and appears in no agenda. Judged
acceptable and "even a good cue to ingest and apply." Agenda visibility
was explicitly ruled out as a concern.

*Second folded defect: no =:CREATED:=.* The template writes only =:ID:=,
against the project's own rule that every heading creation stamps
=:CREATED:=. A bare heading needs it *more*, not less — with no keyword
and no creation entry until ingestion, the property is the only record
that the heading exists and when.

*Third folded defect: capture can only target a file, not a position.*
=(file <file-spec>)= is the least specific target org offers, so
everything lands at top level in whatever file
=claude-code-ide-org-capture-file= names — and that defcustom is =nil=,
so it currently falls through to =org-default-notes-file=,
=~/org/notes.org=, outside the repo entirely. Setting the defcustom alone
would just aim the imprecision at a better file.

*Corrected 2026-08-11, by =org_outline= on its first real call.* This
paragraph previously claimed all three defects were latent because
=~/org/notes.org= contained *zero* captured headings and the tool had
"never been used in anger." Both statements were false, and the error
was methodological: the check was =grep -c '^\* TODO '=, which matches
only the =TODO= keyword, so a captured heading later moved to =DOING=
slipped past it. The structural index showed it immediately — which is
the case [[id:9f3c35c7-0229-4cd6-908b-779267ec9b97][9f3c35c7]] was built
for, arriving about an hour after that heading was written.

*Retracted the same day, one revision later.* The correction above then
over-claimed in the other direction, asserting "the tool has been used
and the damage is real." One heading was observed in =~/org/notes.org=
carrying =:ID:= =780d807e-d441-452f-a407-520225efb3c6= and nothing else
— no =:CREATED:=, top-level, exactly the template's shape — and that
shape was read as proof of provenance. It is not: a hand-made heading, a
heading predating the =:CREATED:= convention, or =org-id-get-create= on
something existing all produce the same thing. The user, asked, described
the file's contents as "pure noise" and cleared it; =notes.org= is now
empty and, being outside the repo, has no git history to re-examine. So
whether =org_capture= has *ever* produced a heading is unresolved and now
unresolvable from evidence.

Worth stating plainly because the paragraph above and this one are the
second and third revisions of the same claim within an hour, all three
correcting in the same direction — asserting more than the evidence
carried. The underlying move each time was filling an unknown with a
plausible value instead of marking it unknown.

*What holds independent of that file's contents:* =notes.org= is in
=org-agenda-files= and =claude-code-ide-org--tracked-files= — still true
now that it is empty — so it is a live capture target that this
project's own tools read, and the earlier claim that it was invisible to
them was wrong on structural grounds, not evidential ones.

*A scope gap that survives the retraction, because it is deductive
rather than evidential.*
[[id:9d2fcdad-9bf7-47b6-8018-223b13ec4577][9d2fcdad]] deleted all 49
=:SESSIONS:= drawers and reports none remaining — true of TODO.org and
DONE.org, 0 each. But that sweep ran over the *repo's* files, and
=notes.org= is tracked without being in the repo, so it was never in
scope. A drawer was in fact observed there before the file was cleared;
even without that observation the gap stands, since the two file sets
differ and nothing reconciles them. Any future sweep over "our org
files" inherits the same blind spot, and =org-agenda-files= is the set
that matters, not the working tree.

Counting them needs an anchored =^:SESSIONS:$=: a bare
=grep -c ':SESSIONS:'= reports *56* in TODO.org by matching prose
mentions of the drawer, which reads as a failed retirement. Exactly the
trap the org skill already warns about — prefer =org-element-map= over
line regexps so a drawer name in prose is never matched as a drawer.

*Placement design, settled with the user 2026-08-11.* An optional target
accepting either an =:ID:= or a refile-style outline path
(=TODO.org/Skill logic=), resolved through the vocabulary
=org-refile-get-targets= already produces — not a new category concept,
and not an ad-hoc exception to "IDs, never titles." The level-1
categories carry no =:ID:= by deliberate convention, so an outline path
is the *only* way to address them; that they are few, stable,
human-curated and never refiled is what makes titles safe here
specifically. Omitting the target keeps today's append-at-top-level
behaviour, which is the honest fallback for "placement unknown" and
removes any need for an inbox heading.

*Implemented 2026-08-11.* The template now writes =* %i= with =:ID:= and
=:CREATED:= and no keyword, and =org_capture= takes an optional
=target=. =:CREATED:= is formatted in elisp rather than via the
template's =%U= escape, for the reason
[[id:cdcc065c-628d-4350-b1ba-fcf686e9c4c1][cdcc065c]] gave: an escape
that fails to expand leaves a literal =%U= behind and still satisfies a
naive is-the-property-present check.

*Deviation from the placement design above, deliberate.* The plan said
the target would accept "a refile-style outline path
(=TODO.org/Skill logic=), resolved through the vocabulary
=org-refile-get-targets= already produces." What shipped accepts an
=:ID:= or the *exact title of a top-level category*. The reason is that
refile-target strings escape =/= inside headings as =\\/= — this file
has a heading containing =NEXT/TODO= that renders as =NEXT\\/TODO= —
so splitting a path on =/= is wrong in a way that would only surface on
particular headings. Matching a level-1 title exactly sidesteps the
parsing entirely and covers both real cases: categories, which have no
=:ID:=, and any ID-bearing parent. Full path support can come later if a
level-2 target is ever wanted; nothing needs it today.

An unknown target *errors* rather than falling back to the end of the
file. A caller that named a destination and silently got a different one
is worse off than one that got an error.

*Still outstanding, and not Claude's to do:*
=claude-code-ide-org-capture-file= is a defcustom set in
=~/.config/doom/config.el=, outside this repo, and it is still =nil= —
so captures continue to land in =org-default-notes-file=. The tool is
correct; the target is not yet pointed at this project. That one line is
the user's to write.

*Verified:* =bin/test= 167/167 — three existing capture tests updated to
the new contract (they asserted =^\* TODO= and a return string ending at
the =:ID:=), six new ones covering the absent keyword, the =:CREATED:=
stamp and its =%U= trap, both target forms, the refusal, and the
omitted-target append. Live against a scratch capture file, all three
paths behaved: =Tooling= demoted the entry to =**= beneath that
category, an omitted target appended at =*=, and a bad target returned
an error with nothing written.

*Scope note: capture is a minority path in this project.* Nine headings
were created on 2026-08-11 and every one was placed correctly by text
editing; =org_capture= was not used once. Its pitch — quick-add without
reading the file — does not survive a house style where headings carry
bodies, cross-links and =:BLOCKER:=s. It still earns its keep for
[[id:7fa68d5c-b6c0-43df-85c6-764b1d56511f][7fa68d5c]]'s no-Emacs
fallback, for a passing mention in a session about something else, and
for immediate =:ID:= registration. Build the smallest thing that makes
it correct, not a parameter surface sized as though it were the primary
way headings get made.

** DONE Add the (t!) logging marker to TODO in the #+TODO: line :code:
:PROPERTIES:

:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 15:52]
:END:

- State "DONE"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-11 Tue 16:18] \\
  TODO(t!) marker added to line 1

TODO.org line 1 carried logging markers on every keyword *except* the
first:

: #+TODO: TODO NEXT(n!) PLANNING(p!) DOING(d!) WAIT(w@/!) MAYBE(m!) | DONE(D!) CANCELLED(c@)

So entering =TODO= was the one transition org logged nothing for. Almost
certainly deliberate and correct when the markers were adopted: every
task starts at =TODO=, so logging it was pure noise.

[[id:47c027d2-7b5a-4de0-baf4-f992f3b814bd][47c027d2]] inverts that.
Under bare-heading capture, entering =TODO= *is* the creation event —
the single entry the timeline most needs — and it was the only one that
would silently not be written. Confirmed by direct test against the real
header: bare → =NEXT= and bare → =DOING= log; bare → =TODO= did not, and
no error was raised.

*Done 2026-08-11:* =TODO= → =TODO(t!)= on line 1. One character. The
blast radius is every future =TODO= transition gaining a =:LOGBOOK:=
line, which is the point.

** DONE Check for a modified Emacs buffer before text-editing a tracked org file :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       0308e7d7-06ea-48fb-8579-6a5a43277674
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 16:22]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 18:16]--[2026-08-11 Tue 18:28] =>  0:12
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 17:34]--[2026-08-11 Tue 17:36] =>  0:02
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-11 Tue 16:47] \\
  precondition documented and snippet verified
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-11 Tue 16:45] \\
  add modified-buffer precondition to org skill
- <2026-08-11 Tue 17:34>--<2026-08-11 Tue 17:36>
- <2026-08-11 Tue 18:16>--<2026-08-11 Tue 18:28>
:END:

Heading creation, tagging and prose edits are text operations by design
— Claude writes the =.org= file on disk directly, and only clock/state
changes go through Emacs. That division is deliberate and still right.
What it lacks is a precondition: nothing checks whether Emacs is holding
a *modified* buffer for the file about to be edited.

*Instance three, 2026-08-11, and the first one Claude caused.* Six new
headings and three body edits were written to TODO.org on disk while
Emacs held a buffer that had been modified — and therefore stale — for
hours. =global-auto-revert-mode= is on but deliberately refuses to
revert a modified buffer, so it had silently stopped tracking the file.
Discovered only because an unrelated =org-id-find= on a
just-written =:ID:= came back nil. Had that not happened, the divergence
would have persisted until something saved or reverted.

Only four characters of the buffer were genuinely unsaved — =maye=,
stray keystrokes appended to a =CLOCK:= duration, exactly the accident
=buffer-read-only= is normally set to prevent. So the resolution was
cheap. It was cheap by luck: any real unsaved work in that buffer would
have forced a genuine three-way merge between disk, buffer, and intent.

*What already guards this, and why it is not enough.* =config.el='s
audit hashing reads straight off disk precisely to catch buffer/disk
divergence ("bit this project twice already", =config.el= ~line 90), but
it detects *after* a mutation rather than preventing one.
=basic-save-buffer= does check =verify-visited-file-modtime= and prompt
=yes-or-no-p= before overwriting a changed file (verified), so
=--review-apply-item='s per-item =save-buffer= cannot silently clobber —
but a prompt is the one thing that must never appear in a
non-interactive path, and it is only reachable at all because apply has
a human present.

*Proposed shape:* the file-level analogue of the reload-precondition
rule in the user's global CLAUDE.md — before text-editing a tracked
=.org= file, establish that Emacs is not holding unsaved changes to it,
and stop and say so if it is. Cheap to check
(=(buffer-modified-p (get-file-buffer f))=), and the answer is the
finding when it comes back non-nil. Belongs in the org skill next to the
existing read-only guidance, which covers the adjacent case (buffer
read-only) but not this one.

*Worth deciding alongside:* whether =global-auto-revert-mode='s refusal
to revert modified buffers should be surfaced somewhere. It is correct
behaviour that produces a silent failure mode — a file the user believes
is auto-reverting quietly stops the moment a stray keystroke marks the
buffer modified.

*Done 2026-08-11.* Added "Before editing: check for an unsaved buffer" to
the org skill, placed ahead of "Editing & transforming" because it is a
precondition to all editing rather than a technique. It gives the
three-field =get-file-buffer=/=buffer-modified-p=/
=verify-visited-file-modtime= check, states that =:modified= non-nil means
*stop and tell the user* — never revert (discards their work) and never
save (discards yours) — and records the incident above as the motivating
case. The auto-revert question folded in there rather than going
elsewhere: the section explains why a modified buffer silently stops
tracking, which is the whole reason the check is needed.

*Verified by running the documented snippet*, not by reading it: against
an open unmodified buffer =(:open t :modified nil :in-sync t)=, and
against a path with no buffer =(:open nil ...)= with no error. That second
case exposed a genuine documentation bug — with no buffer the check
returns =:in-sync nil=, which reads as "out of sync" when it means "not
applicable" — so the text now says to read =:open= first and treat the
other fields as meaningless when it is nil. A snippet that alarms on the
common case would have been worse than no check at all.

*Not tested, and not worth forcing:* whether the skill's own description
causes this section to be consulted at the right moment. That is
trigger-matching, which this project's engineering practices explicitly
exempt from deterministic testing. What is verified is that the content
is correct when read.

** TODO org_capture's no-target fallback writes a level-1 heading, violating the category convention
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       97696fc2-f07b-4586-b280-de6a7d26fb87
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 21:25]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-11 Tue 21:26] \\
  fallback placement defect found by using the tool
:END:

[[id:47c027d2-7b5a-4de0-baf4-f992f3b814bd][47c027d2]] gave =org_capture=
an optional =target= and kept =(file <spec>)= as the fallback when it is
omitted — reasoned at the time as "the honest answer for placement
unknown," which append-at-end-of-file is, in general.

It is wrong for the file capture now points at. Appending to TODO.org
means a *level-1* heading, and this project reserves those for
categories: pure structure, no keyword, no tags, no properties, per
CLAUDE.md's "Top-level headings" rule. So every no-target capture would
produce a heading violating the convention on three counts at once, to
be refiled by hand — the manual step the tool exists to remove.

Nothing has landed wrong yet: both real captures named a target. Found
by using the tool rather than by review.

*Options, none chosen:*
- *Require =target=.* Simplest and most honest — refuse rather than
  guess. Costs the one case the fallback was for: a passing mention
  whose right home genuinely is not known.
- *Fall back to a designated category* rather than the file. Keeps
  capture always-succeeds, at the price of a setting to choose and an
  inbox-shaped thing arriving through the back door, which
  =47c027d2='s discussion rejected at the front.
- *Make the fallback file-aware*: append at top level only where that is
  meaningful, require a target where the file has the category
  convention. Most correct, least obvious, and needs a way to know which
  files have the convention.

Worth deciding alongside
[[id:bd482c92-a71a-4d7d-88df-47678ee94c79][bd482c92]], since this only
bites once the capture target is this project's own file — which is
exactly what that heading makes permanent.

*Second, smaller defect seen in the same capture:* the entry is inserted
with no blank line separating it from the preceding body text or the
following heading, unlike every hand-written heading in this file. Fixed
by hand here. Probably wants =:empty-lines-before 1= on the template.

** TODO org_outline's active_only orphans children of finished headings :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       98908aff-63cc-4b71-b90b-817da361a23b
:CREATED:  [2026-08-12 Wed 16:06]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-12 Wed 16:07] \\
  found while documenting the tool's own response format
:END:

Indentation is the *only* thing =org_outline='s output says about
parentage: =claude-code-ide-org--outline-line= emits two spaces per
absolute =org-current-level=, and =org-map-entries= emits in document
order. A reader reconstructs the tree as "parent = nearest preceding
line with a smaller indent," which holds only while every intermediate
ancestor is present.

=active_only= breaks that. The filter lives in =--outline-line=, which
returns nil for a DONE/CANCELLED heading — it does *not* skip the
subtree in =--outline-map=, so that heading's still-active descendants
are emitted anyway, at their unchanged absolute indent. Verified
2026-08-12 against a scratch file:

: * Category
: ** DONE Finished parent
: *** TODO Active child

returns

: Category
:     TODO Active child

The indent jumps two levels, and a reader re-parents the child onto its
*grandparent* with nothing in the output marking that it did. Silent
wrong structure, not a visible gap.

*Not currently observable in this project's own files*, which is why it
took this long to notice: TODO.org's level-1 headings are categories
carrying no TODO keyword, so nothing on the path from a category to a
level-2 task can ever be filtered out. All 60 lines of a full-depth
=active_only= run were checked and no indent jump occurs. That is a
property of the top-level-heading convention, not a guarantee from the
format — the bug is one three-level task tree away from biting.

=max_depth= is safe by contrast: it only ever drops the deepest lines,
truncating leaves rather than severing an ancestor.

*Options, none chosen:*
- *Emit the elided ancestors anyway*, unfiltered, so the path stays
  intact. Keeps reconstruction sound and costs a few lines the caller
  asked not to see.
- *Re-indent survivors to their visible depth*, so indent means "depth
  in the returned tree" rather than absolute level. Cheapest output,
  but contradicts the deliberate absolute-level decision in
  [[id:9f3c35c7-0229-4cd6-908b-779267ec9b97][9f3c35c7]] that makes
  =max_depth= mean the same thing for a file and for a subtree.
- *Skip the whole subtree* when a heading is filtered out. Simplest
  rule, and the most likely to surprise — an active child of a DONE
  parent is exactly the thing =active_only= should be showing.

*Related, same output, separate defect:* in a multi-file call the
per-file label is a bare =file-name-nondirectory= at column 0,
structurally identical to a level-1 heading, which by this project's
convention also carries no keyword, no =:ID:= and no tags. Nothing but
"it looks like a filename" distinguishes them, so a reader that treats
the label as a heading turns each file into one spurious root. Worth
fixing in the same pass, since both are the same class of bug: the
format asserting less structure than the reader needs.

* Clock lifecycle & visibility

Everything here is specifically about the clock subsystem's
correctness and observability, as opposed to the general TODO-state
machine above — three different angles on "make sure the clock is
never silently wrong."

** TODO Container headings should change state without opening a clock :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       ab75d6d2-0e59-405d-92c6-2c67488db133
:CREATED:  [2026-08-12 Wed 11:25]
:END:

Raised by the user 2026-08-12, on moving
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] to =DOING= because
some of its children had become active — which is the right thing to
express, and should happen whenever a child goes active. The problem is
what came with it: =claude-code-ide-org--trigger-auto-clock-in= fires on
*any* transition to =DOING=/=PLANNING=, hand-edits explicitly included,
so the toggle also opened a clock on the epic. That interval was
unwanted and was deleted by hand.

*Org already does the roll-up, natively.* Measured on this very epic
before the deletion — the clocktable read =14:52= for the parent row,
the ten children summed to =13:44=, and the =1:08= difference is exactly
the epic's own two CLOCK lines. So "activity rolls up to the parent in
reporting" needs no configuration; it is what =:maxlevel= does. The
consequence is the actual argument: a parent's own time is *additive,
inside the same total*, so it cannot be told apart from the children's
sum once rolled up. Clocking a container does not produce a roll-up — it
adds a second, differently-meaning quantity to one that already existed.

*The counter-precedent, and why it does not transfer.* norang's org-GTD
setup ([[https://doc.norang.ca/org-mode.html]]) deliberately puts time on
projects: clocking out of a subtask *cascades up* to the parent so
unattributable time lands somewhere. Two differences. It is a *move* of
one running clock, never a second one minted alongside the children's.
And it exists to catch time no subtask claims — a problem this design
already answers elsewhere, since every interval is attributed to the
heading a session named and unattributed guideposts have
[[id:3d0487f4-b8f8-451c-88bf-ab537663cbd0][3d0487f4]].

*Proposed rule, narrower than "never clock a parent."* Parent clocking
should be *deliberate, never automatic*. The epic's existing =1:08= is
real epic-level work — planning on 08-06, revising the plan against what
shipped on 08-11 — and a blanket "only clock leaves" would discard that
category. So: exempt container headings from the auto-clock-in trigger,
and leave manual =org_clock_in= on a parent available for genuine
coordination work.

*Detecting a container, org-natively:* norang's lazy definition — a
heading with at least one child carrying a TODO keyword. No new
property, no list to maintain.

*Consequence for CLAUDE.md:* "any transition *to* =DOING= or =PLANNING=
must open a clock" acquires a stated exception, which belongs with
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]]'s rewrite rather
than as a separate edit.

*Two things observed while diagnosing this, both worth their own
attention and neither fixed here:*

1. *Deleting the open CLOCK line desynced the live clock* —
   =org-clock-marker= slid onto the *next*, already-closed line
   (=[2026-08-11 Tue 11:10]--[11:13] =>  0:03=), while =org-clocking-p=
   stayed =t=. Any subsequent =org-clock-out= — Emacs quit and suspend
   both run one — would have closed a clock at that marker and corrupted
   a good interval. Note =org-clock-cancel= is *not* the recovery: it
   deletes a region relative to that same wrong marker. Cleared by
   resetting =org-clock-marker=/=org-clock-hd-marker= in place, touching
   no file. This is
   [[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][53b0047d]] observed live,
   and evidence it is not merely theoretical.
2. *=org-log-into-drawer= is nil*, so org's native state-change logging
   lands *outside* =:LOGBOOK:= — visible on b5f7c5c7, whose =DOING= note
   sits above the drawer while older entries sit inside it. Applied
   transitions do land inside, so the two paths disagree. Relevant
   because native logging working correctly is the entire premise of the
   apply design.

   *Resolved 2026-08-12, elsewhere.* Fixed by adding =#+STARTUP:
   logdrawer= to =TODO.org= and =DONE.org=, which sets the variable
   buffer-locally and leaves a user's other org files alone. The
   documentation half belongs to
   [[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]], with drift #4,

   someone else's machine" question it opened is

Distinct from the existing "Known cosmetic quirk" in CLAUDE.md about
=:SESSIONS:= vs =:LOGBOOK:='s *relative drawer position* (which drawer
appears first in the property area) — this is about the entry order
*within* =:SESSIONS:= itself.
