** MAYBE Direct file writes to a live org buffer can desync org-clock-marker :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a
:CREATED:  [2026-08-04 Tue 18:27]
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/clock-marker-desync-guard.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-04, hit live during this session's TODO.org commit-
splitting work: writing TODO.org's content directly via =git
hash-object=/=update-index= (bypassing Emacs) and relying on
=global-auto-revert-mode= to pick up the change desynced Emacs's
*in-memory* clock state from the file text. =org-clock-marker= kept
pointing at its old raw character offset, which after the rewrite
landed on an unrelated =:SESSIONS:= line instead of the =CLOCK:= line
— =org_clock_out= then failed outright with "Clock start time is
gone", and the statusline (which reads live =org-clocking-p=/
=org-clock-marker= state, not the file text — see
=claude-code-ide-org--statusline-task-string=) kept showing "clocked
in" even after a text-level recovery via
=claude-code-ide-org-close-open-interval= had already closed the
=CLOCK:= line on disk.

Worked around manually this time: reset
=org-clock-marker=/=org-clock-hd-marker=/=org-clock-start-time=
directly via =emacsclient=, then called
=claude-code-ide-org--clock-status-hook-out= by hand since normal
=org-clock-out= never got far enough to fire it. The existing "Stale
interval recovery" design (CLAUDE.md) only repairs the *text* side of
a stale interval (crash/shutdown case, where Emacs's own in-memory
state is simply gone after a restart) — it has no equivalent for *this*
case, where Emacs is still running but its live clock state has gone
stale relative to a file it didn't itself edit. Whether the real fix
is "never write live org files outside Emacs, full stop" (a
process/discipline fix, not code) or something more automatic (e.g.
detecting the marker/text mismatch and self-healing) is a question for
planning.

*Second, distinct incident, 2026-08-05*: on
[[id:4cda6bf7-441b-47b2-b3d5-b8c7e7a207a5][Show the TODO keyword, not just clocked in/out, in the statusline]],
after a cross-session clock resume (see the =d150c02e= sibling below)
followed by a proper =org_clock_out=, the =:LOGBOOK:= briefly
contained a genuinely malformed line — bare =CLOCK:= with *no*
timestamp at all — sitting between two valid entries, before
=claude-code-ide-org-consolidate-history= rebuilt the drawer and it
disappeared (the arithmetic of what remained checks out, so nothing
was silently lost, but *why* a timestamp-less =CLOCK:= line got
written in the first place is unconfirmed — a race between two
near-simultaneous writers to the same drawer is the working
hypothesis, not proven). A second, structurally different way the
same "shared mutable file" pressure can corrupt =:LOGBOOK:=, worth
folding into whatever planning addresses this cluster.

Also worth noting for whoever plans =4cda6bf7='s own fix: swapping
=status-label= for =org-get-todo-state= *at the clock marker* is
still marker-dependent — it inherits this exact desync mode (a stale
TODO keyword shown instead of a stale in/out label), it doesn't
route around it.

*Kept and narrowed 2026-08-13, by the user's decision.* Reviewed against
its two partial owners rather than as keep-or-cancel, because that is
what it actually is: a gap between them.

*What is retired.* The original =org_clock_out= failure is unreachable —
that function opens and closes nothing since
[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]]'s cutover. So is
the concurrent-writer =CLOCK:= corruption:
=claude-code-ide-org-consolidate-history= has no production caller left,
only tests.

*What survives, and is the whole of this heading now.* Two surfaces,
each only partly owned elsewhere:
1. =--trigger-auto-clock-in= (config.el:1778, registered :1941) still
   opens a real clock on *any* transition to =DOING=/=PLANNING=,
   including a human's own =C-c C-t=. Apply suppresses it via the
   re-entrancy guard; a hand-edit does not.
   [[id:ab75d6d2-0e59-405d-92c6-2c67488db133][ab75d6d2]] covers
   *container* headings only, so leaf hand-edits remain.
2. =--statusline-task-string= (config.el:659) still reads
   =org-clocking-p=/=org-clock-marker=/=org-clock-history= directly.
   [[id:290b6fc5-3ebb-4827-9a97-5a3542e354b6][290b6fc5]] is scoped to
   the statusline's *data source*, not to marker desync generally.

Cancel this and the uncovered middle belongs to nobody. The deciding
fact against cancelling is that the desync was observed *live on
2026-08-12*, after the cutover, and recorded on =ab75d6d2= — not a
theoretical residue.

*Newly established 2026-08-13, and it raises this heading's stakes.*
Reading =org-clock.el= for
[[id:803314aa-eda9-4d48-9e43-b3eb4802a4e8][803314aa]] found that
=org-clock-out= signals =(error "Clock start time is gone")=
(org-clock.el:1841) exactly when =org-clock-marker='s line is no longer
a =CLOCK:= line — i.e. precisely this heading's desync. That is the same
error incident 1 above recorded. So this desync is not merely a display
problem: it is the reachable trigger for apply's fabricated-interval
bug. The two headings are one causal chain, and this one is upstream.

** CANCELLED session-resume blindly inherits any session's last-clocked heading :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       582cc7f4-41a2-4666-ad3f-1b76b459147e
:CREATED:  [2026-08-04 Tue 18:27]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "CANCELLED"  from "MAYBE"      [2026-08-12 Wed 17:37] \\
  superseded by feba67eb's cutover; see e51d6ba1
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/session-resume-continuity-guard.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-04, observed live and confirmed with real evidence,
not just a one-off guess: immediately after deliberately clocking out
(via the sibling heading above's manual recovery, with no task
actually being worked on — this conversation was pure "care and
feeding" of the project plan, not implementation work), the very next
prompt silently clocked back in on the unrelated =PLANNING TODO state=
heading (ID b95b9fba-f78e-48fe-8546-988709cce309). Root cause:
=session-resume= (the =UserPromptSubmit= hook) unconditionally calls
=org-clock-in-last=, which resumes whichever heading is most recent in
the *global, shared* =org-clock-history= — with no check on whether
*this session* is the one that put it there.

This is broader than "no task in view this turn," and confirmed to
also bite genuinely concurrent sessions, not just idle/maintenance
ones: a =ps= check during this same conversation found two real
=claude= CLI processes running concurrently (separate terminals), and
the =PLANNING TODO state= heading's own =:SESSIONS:= history showed a
*different* session ID pausing/resuming its clock at one point — not
because that session was doing =PLANNING=-state work (confirmed:
=b95b9fba= appears nowhere in =config.el=, so no implementation code
for that feature has landed from *any* session), but almost certainly
that session's own ordinary turn-boundary hook grabbing the same
shared global clock via the same blind =org-clock-in-last= call.
Because the clock is one global resource, *any* session's resume can
silently inherit and misattribute time to whatever heading happens to
sit at the head of =org-clock-history=, regardless of who set it
=DOING= or whether anyone is actually touching it. The existing
session-id-ownership fix (see "Concurrent Claude Code sessions share
one global org-clock", ID 337f7fb2-b9e9-4c02-82dd-d88e60df364b) only
guards a clock that's *actively running* from being paused/stolen by a
different session mid-flight — it does nothing to stop an *idle*
clock from being resumed onto the wrong heading by whichever session's
=UserPromptSubmit= happens to fire next.

Concrete fix direction (sharper than originally captured): make
=session-resume= conditional on session continuity — only call
=org-clock-in-last= if the clock's most recent close (the last
=:SESSIONS:= =Paused= entry, or the session-id already recorded by the
337f7fb2 work) belongs to *this same session's* ID; otherwise leave it
clocked out and require an explicit =org_clock_in= once real work
actually starts on some heading.

Related, same "single shared global clock" theme, distinct root
causes — worth planning together but not assuming they're one fix:
[[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][Direct file writes to a live org buffer can desync org-clock-marker]]
(external writes corrupting Emacs's in-memory marker) and
[[id:d150c02e-06fe-4514-bf69-e2cc5d8df4c7][org_clock_in doesn't record clock ownership, unlike session-resume]]
(most clock-start paths never record an owner in the first place, so
this bug's own "idle clock" precondition is the common case, not the
exception).

*Cancelled 2026-08-12 by
[[id:e51d6ba1-cc1d-4745-8aa7-e4b1183a230b][e51d6ba1]].* Superseded by
shipped work, not deferred. =bin/hooks/session-resume= no longer calls
=org-clock-in-last=, or reaches Emacs at all: it appends a bare =resume=
guidepost to the session's own queue file and exits. A guidepost names
no heading, so there is no wrong heading left to inherit — attribution
comes from the surrounding =clock_in=/=clock_out= events at review time.
=claude-code-ide-org-session-pause=/=-resume= were deleted at the
2026-08-11 cutover ([[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]],
commit =220ba78=); the hook script's own header names this heading as
the bug it removed. The failure mode documented here remains the
clearest statement of why that cutover was worth doing.

** CANCELLED org_clock_in doesn't record clock ownership, unlike session-resume :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       d150c02e-06fe-4514-bf69-e2cc5d8df4c7
:CREATED:  [2026-08-05 Wed 14:28]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "CANCELLED"  from "MAYBE"      [2026-08-12 Wed 17:37] \\
  ownership variables deleted at cutover; see e51d6ba1
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/clock-in-ownership-recording.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-05, hit live, then re-confirmed and sharpened
2026-08-05 with a direct empirical check. Original finding: manually
clocking a heading in via the =org_clock_in= MCP tool
(=claude-code-ide-org-clock-in=) logs a plain "Resumed" =:SESSIONS:=
entry with no session-id, and never sets
=claude-code-ide-org--clock-owner-session-id=, unlike
=claude-code-ide-org-session-resume=.

*Actual scope, confirmed by exhaustively grepping every call site*:
=(org-clock-in)= is only ever invoked from two places —
=claude-code-ide-org-clock-in= (line ~362) and
=claude-code-ide-org--trigger-auto-clock-in= (line ~1596, fired on
*any* =DOING=/=PLANNING= transition via =org-trigger-hook=, so also
on every =org_set_todo= call and every hand-edit). Meanwhile
=claude-code-ide-org--clock-owner-session-id= is only ever *set* from
one place: =claude-code-ide-org-session-resume=. So owner-recording
covers exactly one of the three ways a clock can start — the
*resume-after-pause* case — while the more common case (transitioning
a heading straight to =DOING=/=PLANNING=) is unowned by construction.
Checked the =337f7fb2= DONE writeup: it only ever describes threading
=session_id= through =session-pause=/=-resume=, never claims trigger-
hook coverage — so this is an honest original scope gap, not a
regression.

*Live-verified, not just theorized*: while another session had a
genuine =PLANNING= heading actively clocked (ID
4cda6bf7-441b-47b2-b3d5-b8c7e7a207a5, set via =org_set_todo=, the
"proper" path), a direct =emacsclient= query showed
=claude-code-ide-org--clock-owner-session-id= =nil= while
=claude-code-ide-org--planning-owner-session-id= correctly held that
session's ID — same heading, same instant, one guard fully engaged,
the other completely absent. =337f7fb2='s own guard is deliberately
permissive when no owner is recorded ("the correct fallback for the
ordinary single-session case, not merely a stopgap," per its own
docstring) — so this isn't broken protection, it's protection that
was never wired to the common code path.

Sharper fix direction than originally captured: the precedent already
exists in this exact codebase.
=claude-code-ide-org--maybe-record-planning-owner= (a =PostToolUse=
hook matched on =org_set_todo=, reading =session_id= straight from its
own hook payload) already solves the identical problem for
=planning-owner-session-id=. The same hook — or a sibling of it — can
set =clock-owner-session-id= too, whenever the requested state is
=DOING=/=PLANNING=, without needing to instrument the trigger hook or
=org_clock_in= itself. =org_clock_in='s own missing session-id is a
narrower, separate case (no =PostToolUse= payload carries a =state=
field for a bare clock-in call) worth a smaller, second fix alongside
it.

Related, same "single shared global clock" theme:
[[id:582cc7f4-41a2-4666-ad3f-1b76b459147e][session-resume blindly inherits any session's last-clocked heading]]
and
[[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][Direct file writes to a live org buffer can desync org-clock-marker]]
— three distinct root causes in the same subsystem, worth planning
together, not assumed to be one fix.

*Cancelled 2026-08-12 by
[[id:e51d6ba1-cc1d-4745-8aa7-e4b1183a230b][e51d6ba1]].* Both halves are
structurally gone rather than fixed. =--clock-owner-session-id= and
=--planning-owner-session-id= were deleted at the 2026-08-11 cutover
([[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]], commit
=220ba78=) — they survive only inside =config.el='s tombstone comment —
along with =--maybe-record-planning-owner=, the =PostToolUse= hook this
heading proposed reusing. =org_clock_in= now validates an =:ID:= and
opens no clock, so there is no ownership left to record.

The want behind the heading is better served than the fix it proposed:
=bin/hooks/queue-append= stamps =session_id=, =agent_id=, =agent_type=
and =source= on *every* event, and the queue is keyed per session by
filename — so attribution is recorded per event and survives an Emacs
restart, which no global variable did. Caveat worth carrying forward:
as of 2026-08-12 the =agent_id=/=agent_type= fields have never been
populated in 347 events, so that half is designed but unexercised.

** CANCELLED Audit for clock/session bookkeeping noise before committing during a clocking-suppressed session :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       782cda6c-111c-454e-93eb-b2b27012078b
:CREATED:  [2026-08-05 Wed 14:28]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "CANCELLED"  from "MAYBE"      [2026-08-12 Wed 17:37] \\
  folded into the review UI; see e51d6ba1
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/bookkeeping-noise-audit.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-05, raised in conversation: when a session has been
explicitly told to suppress its own clocking for a while (as this one
was), other concurrent sessions' ordinary activity can still write
real =:LOGBOOK:=/=:SESSIONS:= deltas into headings this session
touches or observes — visible throughout this same session (the
=PLANNING TODO state= heading and the notes.org review-task heading
both picked up cross-session clock churn while under the
suppression). The open question: at commit time, should a
clocking-suppressed session specifically double-check that whatever
clock/session bookkeeping deltas are about to be committed are
*genuine* (real, attributable work — its own or another session's)
rather than side-effect noise the suppressed session itself
incidentally observed or triggered, before folding them into a commit
under this session's authorship?

Not yet clear whether this needs new tooling (e.g. a pre-commit diff
review step specific to =:LOGBOOK:=/=:SESSIONS:= hunks) or is purely a
standing-instruction-level judgment call for whoever is about to
commit — same register as other "only the model/user can decide"
rules already in CLAUDE.md. Related to, but broader than, the sibling
heading above (that one is about a specific missing ownership
mechanism; this one is about what to do with the resulting mess at
commit time, regardless of whether that mechanism ever gets fixed).

Also substantial overlap with
[[id:3cb3f955-f10a-47cd-84ab-e629d73ea59d][Org skill: commit a newly-captured TODO immediately, when safe]] —
that heading already raised the same underlying question in the
abstract before this one found a concrete incident. See its own
2026-08-05 update.

*Second concrete incident, 2026-08-06 — a lost interval, not observed
noise, and a root cause none of the recorded incidents cover.* Found by
running exactly the pre-commit audit this heading proposes, during the
Phase 0 verification for
[[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]]. Sequence: clocked
in on =3d576d29= at 22:35; a live =emacsclient= probe set =DOING= on a
scratch heading, which fired
=claude-code-ide-org--trigger-auto-clock-in= and moved the clock into
the scratch buffer; recovered with =org-clock-cancel=, then re-clocked
=3d576d29= at ~22:43 and clocked out at 22:45. Both =org_clock_in= and
=org_clock_out= reported success. The heading ends up with:

: CLOCK: [2026-08-06 Thu 22:35]--[2026-08-06 Thu 22:40] =>  0:05

— and *no* second interval for 22:43-22:45. =:SESSIONS:= brackets the
full arc correctly (=Resumed 22:37= / =Paused 22:45=); only the CLOCK
interval vanished. Roughly two minutes, so materially irrelevant; the
point is the failure mode, not the magnitude.

*Root cause found 2026-08-07, correcting the paragraph above:* nothing
to do with =org-clock-cancel=, and not a bug — it is documented,
deliberate behavior. =claude-code-ide-org-clock-out= calls
=claude-code-ide-org-consolidate-history= on every clock-out, which
rounds each closed interval to the nearest 5 minutes
(=claude-code-ide-org--round-time-to-5-minutes=, config.el:900; ties
round *up*) and then drops anything that becomes zero-length
(=--consolidate-logbook-text=, config.el:991, mirroring org's own
=org-clock-out-remove-zero-time-clocks= intent after rounding). 22:43
rounds to 22:45; 22:45 rounds to 22:45; zero; dropped. The earlier
interval survived only because 22:35→22:41 rounded to 22:35→22:40.

Generalized, the rule is: *any interval shorter than about 2.5 minutes
that does not straddle a 5-minute boundary disappears silently on
clock-out.* That is a much broader exposure than one incident — it
applies to every short clock the tools have ever closed. Whether the
5-minute granularity is still the right trade is now a live question
rather than a settled one, and it is decided for the new architecture
in [[id:32272061-1d78-4726-b13b-90338edb2ba5][32272061]]'s plan: apply
writes exactly the endpoints the human confirms, with no rounding and
no zero-drop.

What still makes it worth recording separately: every other incident
catalogued here is about *cross-session* interference or a desynced
marker. This one is single-session, sequential, and purely a
consequence of this project's own consolidation policy — no
concurrency involved at all. Deliberately not "fixed" by
hand-writing the missing line: CLAUDE.md forbids editing CLOCK entries
directly, and fabricating an interval to make the record look tidy is
the exact "confidently wrong" record
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] exists to
prevent.

Note the incident is also mild evidence *for* this heading's premise:
the audit caught it, and it would otherwise have been committed
unnoticed inside a 268-line documentation diff.

*Cancelled 2026-08-12 by
[[id:e51d6ba1-cc1d-4745-8aa7-e4b1183a230b][e51d6ba1]], along the line
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] predicted:* its
mechanism-approach "likely folds into the new review UI rather than
standing alone." All three parts of the premise dissolved. "A
clocking-suppressed session" has no referent — no session clocks at all
since the 2026-08-11 cutover. =:SESSIONS:= no longer exists, and the
only automatic =:LOGBOOK:= writers left fire from =org-todo=, which now
runs only inside the human-driven apply or a human's own hand-edit. And
the genuine-vs-noise judgment is now made *before* anything lands, in
the review buffer with session and timing visible, which is what this
proposed pre-commit audit was reconstructing after the fact.

The second incident recorded above (the vanished 2-minute interval) was
root-caused separately: =consolidate-history='s 5-minute rounding,
retired in =f0fc4a2=. Apply writes exact confirmed endpoints with
=org-clock-out-remove-zero-time-clocks= bound nil.

The surviving commit-hygiene question is not lost —
[[id:3cb3f955-f10a-47cd-84ab-e629d73ea59d][3cb3f955]] holds the
single-session half and
[[id:2aeb65d6-350e-4c75-811e-4a748044fe2c][2aeb65d6]] the cross-session
one, which found the real hazard to be the shared =.git/index=, not
drawer deltas: a different mechanism from the one hypothesised here.

Discovered 2026-08-06 while committing the
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] Plan link
(commit =727d94a=): =claude-code-ide-org--consolidate-logbook-text=
rebuilds a =:LOGBOOK:= body from parsed =CLOCK:= lines *only* — its
=--parse-clock-lines= keeps an open line and closed intervals and
nothing else — so every other line in the drawer is silently
discarded on each clock-out's consolidation pass. Live casualty,
caught in that commit's own diff: the epic heading's *native*
state-change note

: - State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-06 Thu 12:07] \\
:   Auto-promoted: sole remaining TODO in its sibling group.

was destroyed by the Stop-hook clock-out at 16:50. Restore that exact
line as part of the fix — restoring it any earlier is futile, since
the very next clock-out wipes it again.

Fix shape: preserve non-CLOCK lines verbatim through consolidation
(position among the rebuilt CLOCK lines is cosmetic; survival is
not). Directly load-bearing for
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]: the
review-and-apply command's whole point is writing native state-change
notes and guidepost annotations into =:LOGBOOK:= — worthless if the
next consolidation eats them. Also fresh, concrete evidence for
[[id:782cda6c-111c-454e-93eb-b2b27012078b][782cda6c]] (bookkeeping
noise audit before committing): the noise this time contained silent
data *loss*, not just clutter.

*Verified, not just implemented:* both consolidators now carry
every unrecognized line through verbatim —
=--parse-clock-lines=/=--parse-session-lines= gained an =:other=
channel, emitted after the rebuilt CLOCK block (=:LOGBOOK:=) and
between the collapsed days and the open tail (=:SESSIONS:=). Two
new ERT regressions reproduce the incident's exact drawer shape
(124/124 green via =bin/test=), and after a live reload the
destroyed State "NEXT" note was restored on
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] and survived
a real in-situ =consolidate-history= pass. Scope grew one sibling
during the fix: =:SESSIONS:= consolidation had the same bug class
and was silently eating =Background-planned= write-backs; fixed
and regression-tested in the same pass.

** DONE Concurrent Claude Code sessions share one global org-clock with no session identity :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       337f7fb2-b9e9-4c02-82dd-d88e60df364b
:CREATED:  [2026-07-30 Thu 12:39]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-07-30 Thu 17:30]--[2026-07-30 Thu 17:40] =>  0:10
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/concurrent-session-clock-identity.md][Plan]]

Surfaced 2026-07-30 while starting work on the sibling entry above
(=session-resume= reopening a DONE heading's clock, ID
2e34162a-4720-4d23-bf29-10eb295cd087), with a second concurrent
session active on the Warp MCP proxy bridge task (ID
6a6d5b4e-0327-4578-a44a-356576870ceb). The =Stop=/=UserPromptSubmit=
hooks (=session-pause=/=session-resume=) operated on the single global
Emacs clock and =org-clock-history= with no notion of which session
they belong to — any concurrent session's turn boundary could pause,
resume, or steal another session's running clock.

*Verified, not just implemented:* threaded Claude Code's own
=session_id= (already present on every Stop/UserPromptSubmit hook
payload, previously discarded via =cat >/dev/null=) through
=bin/hooks/session-pause=/=session-resume= — via =jq='s own
JSON-string encoding into the =emacsclient -e= call, not naive shell
interpolation, avoiding any injection risk — down to new optional
=session-id= arguments on
=claude-code-ide-org-session-pause=/=-resume=. A new
=claude-code-ide-org--clock-owner-session-id= records which session's
=session-resume= call most recently opened the running clock;
=session-pause=/=session-resume= now no-op instead of
pausing/stealing a clock owned by a different session. =nil=
session-id (a manual/test call, or omitted from the hook payload)
reproduces the exact original unconditional behavior — not a special
case, the correct fallback for the ordinary single-session case.
=:SESSIONS:= entries also record the session-id when known, directly
visible evidence for verification. Explicitly out of scope:
=clock-status.json= has the same latent single-global-file gap under
concurrent writes — not fixed here.

Deliberately does not attempt true concurrent clocking (org itself
only ever supports one running clock); narrows the failure mode to the
same low-cost "stray interval, self-corrects" category already
accepted for the unrelated "wrong but still active task" case.

=bin/test= passes 85/85 (5 new regression tests: cross-session pause
and resume both correctly blocked and leave the other session's clock
untouched; same-session pause still succeeds; omitting session-id
still bypasses the ownership check entirely; the :SESSIONS: session-id
annotation is present). All 5 pre-existing session-pause/-resume tests
pass unchanged. Also verified live end-to-end through the actual bash
hook scripts (not just the elisp functions directly) against a scratch
heading: simulated two concurrent sessions via fabricated
Stop/UserPromptSubmit JSON payloads piped into
=bin/hooks/session-pause=/=session-resume= — session B's pause and
resume attempts on session A's running clock both correctly no-op'd
with the clock left untouched and still owned by A; session A then
paused its own clock normally, with the owner correctly clearing
afterward.

** DONE org-clock-out-remove-zero-time-clocks silently erases short CLOCK intervals :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       293ac49e-3324-421c-8495-de1a46fc04ac
:CREATED:  [2026-07-30 Thu 14:50]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-07-30 Thu 17:25]--[2026-07-30 Thu 17:30] =>  0:05
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/zero-time-clock-erasure.md][Plan]]

Found live: the sibling heading above (=session-resume= reopening a
DONE heading's clock, ID 2e34162a-4720-4d23-bf29-10eb295cd087) did
real, substantial work — a new function, a regression test — but its
=:LOGBOOK:= was completely empty. Root cause: Doom's =lang/org= module
sets =org-clock-out-remove-zero-time-clocks t= (not anything this
project configured); org-native =org-clock-out= deletes a just-closed
=CLOCK:= line outright based on its *raw* duration, before
=claude-code-ide-org-consolidate-history= (this project's own
rounding+merge+drop logic, which already replicates the same intent
correctly) ever gets a chance to run.

*Verified, not just implemented:* fixed precisely at the conflict
point, not globally — =claude-code-ide-org-clock-out= now let-binds
=org-clock-out-remove-zero-time-clocks= to nil around its own
=(org-clock-out)= call, so org-native's premature deletion never fires
on the Claude-tool/hook-driven path, while a human's own manual
=org-clock-out= keeps org's normal behavior untouched. New regression
test (=claude-code-ide-org-test-clock-out-suppresses-zero-time-removal=)
captures the dynamic value =org-clock-out-remove-zero-time-clocks=
actually has at the moment org-clock.el's own =org-clock-out= runs,
proving it's nil there even with the test's outer binding (simulating
Doom's real override) set to t — direct mechanism verification, since
interval-arithmetic analysis showed a same-minute (the only case org's
raw check fires on) interval rounds to zero either way at minute
resolution, so asserting on final disk content alone can't distinguish
buggy from fixed. =bin/test= passes 80/80. Also confirmed live against
the running Doom Emacs (reload, then the same dynamic-binding capture
against a scratch heading): a same-minute clock-in/out under
=org-clock-out-remove-zero-time-clocks= t completes cleanly with the
interval correctly dropped by this project's own logic, no crash, no
premature deletion.

** DONE Enforce the transition rules :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       39b08ecd-6350-48a2-b169-98535b626ac7
:CREATED:  [2026-07-30 Thu 15:56]
:END:
CLAUDE.md's transition table (TODO→DOING opens a clock, etc.) was pure
prose at one point, with nothing stopping a hand-edit in Emacs, or a
future tool call, from violating it. Recreated 2026-07-30 as a =NEXT=
after being found stripped out of TODO.org's own cleanup with no
subheadings ever added under it — but turned out to already be
implemented and tested in =config.el=/=config-test.el=, just never
reflected back into the roadmap.

*Verified, not just implemented:* =claude-code-ide-org--blocker-clock-running-p=
(on =org-blocker-hook=) denies a DONE transition while that exact
heading's own clock is running, and
=claude-code-ide-org--trigger-auto-clock-in= (on =org-trigger-hook=)
auto-opens a clock the instant any heading becomes DOING — both
registered globally via =with-eval-after-load 'org=, so they catch
every path, not just =claude-code-ide-org-set-todo=. Confirmed the
"Claude-side hook, one step earlier" half of the original design was
never a separate mechanism to build:
=claude-code-ide-org-set-todo= calls plain =org-todo=, which *is* the
function these hooks intercept, so Claude's own tool path already goes
through the same enforcement as a hand-edit — no narrower catch point
exists to add. =config-test.el= has four dedicated regression tests,
including one that bypasses the wrapper entirely (a bare =org-todo=
call) to prove the enforcement lives in org itself. =bin/test= passes
79/79.

** MAYBE Idle-based auto clock-out :code:
Via =org-clock-idle-time=, already built into org — just needs
enabling. Guards against a clock left running because a session
died mid-DOING without ever reaching org_clock_out.

No config.el/config-test.el changes — there's no wall-clock idle to
simulate in ERT's scratch-buffer fixtures.

** MAYBE Coarsen :SESSIONS: to session-level, not turn-level :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CREATED:  [2026-07-28 Tue 11:06]
:END:
:SESSIONS: currently inherits the same Stop/UserPromptSubmit hooks as
:LOGBOOK:, so every turn boundary writes a Paused/Resumed pair — fine
for CLOCK, whose "many short intervals instead of one long one"
granularity is intentional (see the dual-timekeeping design note), but
not what :SESSIONS: was meant to show: the *session-level* bracketing
arc, not per-prompt noise. Wanted instead: a genuine close only at
DOING → DONE with the work tested and committed, at the end of a
working day, or on an explicit signal — sketched as a command like
=/dfd <task>= ("done for the day").

*Open question:* does "end of a working day" need its own detection
(e.g. against =claude-code-ide-org-working-hours=), or is =/dfd=
always a manual call — auto-detecting day boundaries crosses into
guessing user intent, exactly the kind of call CLAUDE.md's existing
rules say only the model/user should make, not a hook.

** MAYBE Consider archives in org_clock_report's scope (agenda-with-archives) :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CREATED:  [2026-07-29 Wed 12:25]
:END:
Deferred out of the tracked-files fix above: once =DONE.org= (or any
archive file) is ever reachable from
=claude-code-ide-org--tracked-files=, retrospective =org_clock_report=
calls without an =:ID:= should account for archived work's clocked
time too — completed tasks' =:LOGBOOK:= history moves into =DONE.org=
on archive, so excluding it undercounts. Not urgent now: =DONE.org=
isn't symlinked into =~/org= today, so it isn't in scope either way.

Org's clocktable machinery already has a built-in mechanism for
exactly this — the =:scope= parameter accepts ='agenda-with-archives=
(or ='file-with-archives=), which follows each agenda file's
=#+ARCHIVE:=/=org-archive-location= automatically. Prefer that over
symlinking =DONE.org= alongside =TODO.org= in =~/org= if/when this
becomes worth building — it keeps the interactive prospective agenda
and the retrospective clock-report scope independently correct
instead of coupled to the same file list.

** MAYBE Worktree-based session partitioning, with a canonical-file caveat :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       2e09adb7-6e29-499c-a40e-98cd2b327cff
:CREATED:  [2026-08-04 Tue 13:29]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-04 Tue 13:30] \\
  Auto-promoted: sole remaining TODO in its sibling group.
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/worktree-session-partitioning.md][Plan]]

Raised in conversation 2026-08-04: can separate Claude Code sessions
work on distinct TODOs simultaneously by each operating in its own
=git worktree= of a project repo? For the *code* side, yes — that's
exactly what worktrees are for, one checkout + branch per session,
no shared-file collisions. Emacs' own uniquify already disambiguates
same-basename buffers at different paths, so that part isn't even in
question.

Where it doesn't extend: =TODO.org=/=DONE.org= themselves must stay a
single canonical copy in the main checkout, not one copy per
worktree. Two failure modes rule out a per-worktree copy: (1) =:ID:=
lookups go through =org-id-locations=, a single global hash table
keyed by ID — a heading =:ID:= duplicated across two on-disk copies
(which a worktree checkout produces automatically) means whichever
copy was scanned last silently wins, with every =org_*= tool
targeting an unpredictable copy; (2) CLOCK/=:SESSIONS:= data written
into a worktree-local copy is invisible to =org_clock_report=/
=org_query=/the agenda until that branch merges, and is lost outright
if the worktree is torn down first — fragmenting exactly the
attention-tracking data the clock exists to keep whole. The single
clock living in one Emacs process reading one canonical file is the
mechanism that enforces "one attention at a time," not a limitation
to route around.

Related: [[id:337f7fb2-b9e9-4c02-82dd-d88e60df364b][Concurrent Claude Code sessions share one global org-clock with no session identity]]
already solved the narrower "which session owns the running clock"
problem this would otherwise collide with. This entry is scoped to
whether/how to formalize worktrees-for-code as a documented pattern
(e.g. in CLAUDE.md's Emacs integration section) alongside that
existing single-canonical-file constraint — not yet decided as worth
building anything for; may just be a documentation note rather than
code.

*Update 2026-08-05:* the core premise here — "the single clock is the
mechanism that enforces one attention at a time, not a limitation to
route around" — is exactly what's being stress-tested by real
concurrent-session activity found this same day:
[[id:582cc7f4-41a2-4666-ad3f-1b76b459147e][session-resume blindly inherits any session's last-clocked heading]],
[[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][Direct file writes to a live org buffer can desync org-clock-marker]],
and
[[id:d150c02e-06fe-4514-bf69-e2cc5d8df4c7][org_clock_in doesn't record clock ownership, unlike session-resume]].
Not reopening the conclusion here — just flagging that if/when this
gets planned, it should be read against that fresh evidence, not just
the original 2026-07-30 reasoning.

** TODO Stale entries in org-clock's persisted history hang the first clock-in after a restart :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       001fb310-69f7-4417-81be-a224a3bf83e0
:CREATED:  [2026-08-10 Mon 11:51]
:END:

Hit live 2026-08-10 while setting =f9f61c04= to =DOING=. The
=org_set_todo= MCP call timed out, while Emacs itself stayed responsive
to =emacsclient -e= -- a recursive edit still runs a command loop, so
the server keeps answering. That combination makes a blocked Emacs look
like a tool fault.

*Mechanism, fully root-caused:* Doom advises =org-clock-in= with
=+org--clock-load-a=, a =:before= advice that lazily calls
=org-clock-load= on the *first* clock-in of an Emacs session.
=org-clock-load= visits every file named in the persisted history
(=org-clock-persist-file=, here
=~/.config/emacs/.local/etc/org-clock-save.el=). That history still
named =/private/tmp/planning-verify/scratch.org=, a scratch file from an
earlier verification session whose directory /tmp reaping had since
removed. Visiting it produced a buffer whose save asked
=Directory .../planning-verify/ does not exist! Create it? (y or n)= --
and a =y-or-n-p= is *not* an error, so no =condition-case= in this
module could have caught it. It simply waited.

*Why it first looked like leaked state:* a separate =emacsclient= query
reported =claude-code-ide-org--auto-clock-in-active= as =t=, which reads
exactly like a re-entrancy guard that failed to unwind. It was not --
the =let=-binding was still live on the blocked call's stack. Reading it
that way is what located the hang inside =--trigger-auto-clock-in=, and
it generalizes: a dynamic binding observed as set from outside means the
frame that bound it has not returned.

*Cleared 2026-08-10*: =abort-recursive-edit=, killed the stale buffer,
filtered =org-clock-history= of markers into nonexistent files, and
called =org-clock-save= to rewrite the persist file from the cleaned
live history. Verified -- the file no longer mentions the path, and the
next =org_clock_in= returned immediately.

*What is NOT fixed, and is the actual task here:* nothing prevents a
recurrence. Any verification pass that clocks into a scratch org file
under /tmp writes that path into the persisted history, and the first
clock-in after the next restart hangs once /tmp is reaped. =bin/test=
runs =emacs --batch -Q= and cannot pollute the real history, but hand
verification in a live Emacs does exactly this -- and
[[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]] established that
live verification is *necessary* here, because batch behaves
differently. Options, unevaluated: prune nonexistent files via an
=org-clock-load= advice; keep live-verification scratch files somewhere
/tmp reaping does not reach; or set =org-clock-persist= to nil and
accept losing clock history across restarts.

Relevant to [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]]: a
hang in the clock path is the exact failure class that refactor exists
to eliminate, and this one survives the cutover untouched, since the
apply command clocks in through the same advised =org-clock-in=.

** DOING Refactor clock/TODO-state driving to an append-only event queue + interactive batch-apply review :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644
:CREATED:  [2026-08-06 Thu 10:47]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 11:10]--[2026-08-11 Tue 11:13] =>  0:03
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-06 Thu 22:35]
CLOCK: [2026-08-06 Thu 15:45]--[2026-08-06 Thu 16:50] =>  1:05
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-06 Thu 12:07] \\
  Auto-promoted: sole remaining TODO in its sibling group.
:END:

[[file:~/.claude/plans/plan-this-task-clever-cupcake.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-06 from conversation, after a frank assessment of
this whole clock/session-identity subsystem concluded the *original*
idea — Claude Code sessions driving live org state (TODO keyword,
clock, =:LOGBOOK:=/=:SESSIONS:= logging) directly and synchronously,
in real time, across concurrent/background sessions — is not bearing
fruit. Evidence, not just a vibe: in roughly a day and a half of real
use this produced two distinct clock-marker desync incidents
([[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][Direct file writes to a
live org buffer can desync org-clock-marker]]), a malformed bare
=CLOCK:= line from concurrent writers, an idle-clock-hijack bug
([[id:582cc7f4-41a2-4666-ad3f-1b76b459147e][session-resume blindly
inherits any session's last-clocked heading]]), a clock-ownership
gap covering 2 of 3 clock-open paths
([[id:d150c02e-06fe-4514-bf69-e2cc5d8df4c7][org_clock_in doesn't
record clock ownership, unlike session-resume]]), a zero-time-clock
erasure bug (=293ac49e=, DONE), a genuine-looking-but-actually-noise
bookkeeping delta needing human cleanup after the fact (commits
=4cc769f=/=bc45342=), and — the proximate trigger for this whole
heading — discovering that
[[id:04d0e7d5-ab6b-4972-925d-d517484c7595][native org state-change
logging]] was implemented, then found live to hang Emacs
indefinitely when triggered non-interactively via =emacsclient -e=,
and so was quietly suppressed for every tool-driven transition ever
since. Root cause, not a string of unrelated bugs: org's clock/
logging model assumes one human, one buffer, one synchronous
keypress at a time. This project's actual workload — concurrent
sessions, background agents, non-interactive =emacsclient= calls, a
global clock paused/resumed across turn boundaries — is the opposite
of that, and every incident above traces back to the same mismatch.
See [[id:c954f650-b92f-4920-9967-ab6c44577b30][Revisit the PLANNING
TODO-state's design and whether it's worth keeping]] (=MAYBE=,
captured the same day) — this heading substantially supersedes that
one's question rather than answering it in place, since a working
version of this redesign would make most of =PLANNING='s current
fragility moot.

*Proposed redesign:* Claude Code sessions stop touching the live
Emacs buffer for anything clock- or state-sensitive. Instead they
append plain, dumb, append-only JSON events to a per-session file
(e.g. =~/.claude/org-updates/<session_id>.jsonl=) — no buffer edits,
no clock mutation, no non-interactive logging trigger, most of it not
even requiring Emacs to be reachable to write. A new interactive
Emacs command reads the accumulated events across all pending session
files, presents them to the human as a reviewable batch (think
=org-agenda='s bulk-mark UI or =magit-status='s staging area, not a
raw diff), and applies the approved subset through org's *real*,
synchronous, single-actor machinery — =org-todo=, =org-clock-in=,
=org-clock-out= — called for real, inside a genuinely interactive
command, so native =!=/=@= logging finally just works instead of
needing to be suppressed.

*Not everything moves* — only the two categories that have actually
caused every incident: TODO-state transitions and clock start/stop.

| Stays direct, as today                                    | Moves to queue + review              |
|------------------------------------------------------------+---------------------------------------|
| =org_query= (read-only)                                    | =org_set_todo= (all states — needed for logging to fire, not just clock states) |
| =org_clock_report= (read-only)                              | =org_clock_in= / =org_clock_out=       |
| =org_refile=, =org_archive=, =org_move_sibling=, =org_sort_children= (never implicated in any incident) | Stop/UserPromptSubmit session-pause/resume signals (become bare timestamp appends — no Emacs call, no owner variable, no cross-session race, since there's no shared live clock left to race on) |
| =org_capture= (heading creation — separate, orthogonal bugs already noted elsewhere) | |
| =org_log_background_plan= (already deliberately clock/state-free — already exactly this pattern) | |

*What this eliminates* (mapped against the evidence above): marker
desync (no writer but the human-driven apply command ever touches
=TODO.org=/=DONE.org= — *false as written, see the correction below*),
the idle-clock-hijack and missing-ownership
bugs (moot — ownership is just the queue filename, no shared clock to
inherit), the malformed-concurrent-write =CLOCK:= corruption (exactly
one writer, one operation at a time), the noise-vs-genuine bookkeeping
judgment call (reviewed *before* anything lands, with session/timing
visible, instead of reconstructed from a git diff after the fact —
[[id:782cda6c-111c-454e-93eb-b2b27012078b][Audit for clock/session
bookkeeping noise before committing during a clocking-suppressed
session]]'s mechanism-approach likely folds into the new review UI
rather than standing alone), and the non-interactive logging hang that
started this whole thread.

*Correction 2026-08-13 to the marker-desync claim above, which is
load-bearing and false.* "No writer but the human-driven apply command
ever touches =TODO.org=/=DONE.org=" was never true and still is not.
=org_capture=, =org_refile=, =org_archive=, =org_move_sibling= and
=org_sort_children= all still write those files directly and
immediately; the org skill text-edits them routinely with Claude Code's
=Edit= tool, which does not go through Emacs at all; git rewrites them
under a live buffer with =global-auto-revert-mode= on; and
=--trigger-auto-clock-in= was deliberately *kept*, so a human's own
=C-c C-t= still opens a clock.

The cutover removed the *clock and state* writers, not the *text*
writers. Marker desync is therefore not eliminated — it was observed
live 2026-08-12, and reading =org-clock.el= on 2026-08-13 showed it is
the reachable trigger for
[[id:803314aa-eda9-4d48-9e43-b3eb4802a4e8][803314aa]]'s fabricated
interval, via =(error "Clock start time is gone")=. Anyone cancelling
[[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][53b0047d]] by citing the
sentence above would be retiring a live bug on the strength of a claim
this heading never established.

*What it costs, honestly:* real-time org state lags until a review pass runs
(mitigate by pointing the statusline at the *queue* files instead of live clock
state — see sub-task below); adds a manual review step to the human's workflow
(keep it cheap — one keybinding); and this **likely obsoletes, in their
currently-planned form**, [[file:~/.claude/plans/clock-marker-desync-guard.md][=53b0047d='s plan]], [[file:~/.claude/plans/session-resume-continuity-guard.md][=582cc7f4='s plan]], and [[file:~/.claude/plans/clock-in-ownership-recording.md][=d150c02e='s
plan]] — each patches a problem this architecture makes structurally impossible
rather than mitigated. Not wasted work: each documents its failure mode
precisely, which is exactly the input this redesign is answering. [[file:~/.claude/plans/sessions-logbook-ordering.md][=2b0988ed='s
plan]] (entry ordering) most likely still applies independent of this refactor,
worth the evaluator's own judgment either way.

*Load-bearing assumption, explicitly flagged for early verification,
not assumed true:* this design only works if the org functions the
apply command calls (=org-clock-in=, =org-clock-out=, whatever drives
=org-add-log-note=/=org-store-log-note=) actually accept — or can be
made to accept — an explicit *retrospective* timestamp, so a batch
applied at 5pm can still backfill a =CLOCK:= interval and a state-
change log note at the times the underlying events actually happened
at 9am, 11am, etc., not "now." If that turns out to be false or only
partially true for some of these functions, significant parts of the
design below need rethinking before implementation, not after.

Nested below as separate sub-tasks, deliberately broken out for
readability rather than left as one undifferentiated blob — each gets
its own =:ID:= and starts as =TODO=; sequencing among them (which
should happen first) is left to whoever evaluates this, not fixed
here, except where a sub-task's own text says otherwise.

Triaged 2026-08-06, later the same day: they no longer all sit at
=TODO=. =c9292ce8= (read-time overlay) is =CANCELLED= as working
against this refactor's own premise; =16160d23= (per-subagent
intervals) is =MAYBE=, blocked on =3d576d29=; =7771fc63= was added for
the stale-interval-recovery feature, whose premise this design removes.
=3d576d29='s clock half is now confirmed answered and its scope
narrowed to the logging half. Each carries its own note; this is just
the index.

*Plan staleness audited 2026-08-11.* This epic's own plan file has been
superseded on most of its concrete decisions by the two later plans, and
nothing in it says so. A reader reaching for it first — which is what an
epic-level plan invites — would be misled on the queue path, the schema,
attribution and apply bookkeeping alike:

| the plan says                                                                   | what was built                                                                |
|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| queue at =<repo>/.claude/org-updates/=                                          | =~/.claude/org-updates/=, global                                              |
| =session_id= as explicit tool arguments                                         | the hook writes it; the tool never sees it                                    |
| missing =session_id= lands in =unattributed.jsonl=, "never dropped"             | the event is dropped outright                                                 |
| every event carries a UUID =event_id=; =applied= events append to the same file | no =event_id= exists; applied state is a sibling =.applied= set               |
| schema =(event_id, heading_id, payload)=                                        | =(ts, kind, id, state, from, note, session_id, agent_id, agent_type, source)= |
| an elisp writer plus =bin/org-queue-append=                                     | shell only, at =bin/hooks/queue-append=                                       |
| retire =--trigger-auto-clock-in=                                                | kept, and suppressed during apply via the re-entrancy guard                   |
| branch =feature/event-queue-review=                                             | =feature/event-queue-format=                                                  |

*And one live contradiction, not merely staleness.* The plan's decision 5
records the =:SESSIONS:= drawer's retirement as *confirmed with the
user*. [[id:9d2fcdad-9bf7-47b6-8018-223b13ec4577][9d2fcdad]], written a
day later, treats the same question as deliberately deferred and
=MAYBE=. Both cannot be current, and the disagreement is load-bearing:
it decides whether a whole subsystem gets deleted.

Until the plan is revised, =event-queue-format.md= and
=review-and-cutover.md= are authoritative where they disagree with it —
they are later and were written against real code.

*** DONE Design the append-only event-queue format and per-session file lifecycle :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       32272061-1d78-4726-b13b-90338edb2ba5
:CREATED:  [2026-08-06 Thu 10:47]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-06 Thu 22:55]--[2026-08-07 Fri 08:20] =>  9:25
- State "DOING"      from "PLANNING"   [2026-08-07 Fri 07:59] \\
  Auto-promoted: ExitPlanMode fired on the owning session.
:END:

[[file:~/.claude/plans/event-queue-format.md][Plan]]

JSONL schema per event kind (=todo=, =clock_in=, =clock_out=,
=pause=, =resume=, at minimum), file naming/location
(=~/.claude/org-updates/<session_id>.jsonl= proposed, not settled),
and lifecycle after a successful apply (delete vs. truncate vs.
append an =applied= marker so a session can keep writing after a
partial drain without needing to know if it was fully consumed).
Crash-safety: a session dying mid-stream should leave its queue file
harmless and inert, never corrupt, never lost.

Note added 2026-08-06: =pause=/=resume= events are not just historical
bookkeeping — per
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]'s design, they
become the *guideposts* a human uses to reconstruct their own real
=CLOCK:= intervals at review time. Their timestamp density directly
determines how useful that reconstruction is, so keep emitting one
per real turn boundary (matching today's =Stop=/=UserPromptSubmit=
granularity) rather than coarsening them for this design's sake —
coarser guideposts make the human's own reconstruction job harder,
not easier.

*Open question to settle here, raised 2026-08-06: do =clock_in=/
=clock_out= events survive at all for human-attended sessions?* Follow
the design through and they are demoted from timing records to bare
*intent markers*. For a human session the interval can't be applied
from them anyway — [[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]
requires the human to draw it from =pause=/=resume= guideposts, which
are strictly denser and better-founded than a =clock_in= event. The one
place a =clock_in=/=clock_out= *pair* still carries authoritative
timing is the subagent path, and that path is now deferred
([[id:16160d23-273b-4510-858e-db18508d04a0][16160d23]], =MAYBE=), so
nothing in v1 consumes them as timing data at all. Two live options:
1. *Keep them as intent markers.* Their remaining job is heading
   attribution — telling the review UI which heading Claude believed it
   was on, so guideposts can be grouped when several tasks interleave
   inside one session. The guidepost stream alone does not solve that,
   which is the real argument for keeping them.
2. *Drop them for v1.* =org_set_todo= → =DOING= already carries the same
   intent, and pause/resume already carry the timing. Smaller event
   schema, and it lets CLAUDE.md's state-transition table lose the
   "always call =org_clock_in=" rule entirely, since there'd be no
   clock to open.

Leaning (1) on the interleaving argument, but genuinely undecided and
worth settling before the schema is written rather than after.

*Related, decide alongside it:* even as queue-appends, the MCP tools
still execute inside Emacs — they're served by the Emacs tools server
(=.mcp.json=, =claude-code-ide-emacs-tools-setup=), so =org_clock_in=
reaches Emacs and appends a line there. Harmless (no buffer mutation,
no clock state), but it is *not* the "no Emacs required to write"
property the parent heading claims — only the hooks get that, per
[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]]'s "no
=emacsclient= call at all." Whether the clock/state tools should also
write the queue file directly, bypassing the tools server so they work
with Emacs down, is unrecorded elsewhere and belongs to this sub-task's
file-lifecycle design.

*Both resolved during planning, 2026-08-07.* =clock_in=/=clock_out=
events are kept, on the basis the user set out: they exist to inform the
human's composition of =CLOCK:= entries at review time, contributing
only *active* timestamps to =:LOGBOOK:=, subject to aggregation at
ingestion so review stays manageable. The decisive technical argument is
attribution — =pause=/=resume= are heading-less, and a return to a
heading that never left =DOING= emits no =todo= event, so without them
every subsequent guidepost silently attributes to the wrong heading.
On the write path: the *hook* writes and the *tool* validates, since
=session_id= only ever appears on hook stdin and never reaches an MCP
tool.

*Implementation notes (2026-08-07):* shipped the schema, the writer and
the reader. =bin/hooks/queue-append= is the single shared writer, called
with the event kind as =$1= and the hook payload on stdin; it builds the
line entirely with =jq -n= (same reasoning as =session-pause='s existing
comment), refuses a =session_id= that is not a plain filename component,
and drops any =todo=/=clock_in=/=clock_out= whose =tool_response= begins
with ="Error:"= — =--at-id= returns error strings rather than throwing,
so that is the only signal a bad =:ID:= never resolved. On the Emacs
side, a new "Event queue" section adds
=claude-code-ide-org-queue-directory= and
=claude-code-ide-org-guidepost-gap-threshold= (default 15 minutes,
explicitly a starting guess) plus =--queue-events=,
=--queue-events-by-id=, =--aggregate-guideposts= and the watermark
functions. Draining never touches the =.jsonl=: a sibling
=<session-id>.applied= watermark records progress, so a session that is
still appending is never raced.

*Verified, not just implemented:* =bin/test= passes 134/134 (124
pre-existing + 10 new ERT tests) covering round-trip of all five kinds,
skipping torn/malformed/unknown-kind lines without failing the file,
watermark filtering including a session that keeps appending after a
partial drain, atomicity of the watermark write, the =A → B → A=
attribution case, cross-session isolation, gap clustering at two
thresholds, and that a 2-minute span survives where
=--consolidate-logbook-text= drops it (asserted as a test, not a
comment). =bin/queue-append-test= adds 20 shell assertions on the writer
— field mapping per kind, ="Error:"= suppression, missing/traversing
=session_id=, unparseable payload, append accumulation, per-session
files, always-exit-0. Also ran a cross-boundary pass neither suite
covers: the real hook script wrote a nine-event =A → B → A= stream which
the real Emacs reader then attributed correctly, drained 9 → 0 via the
watermark, and left the queue file intact at nine lines.

*Deviations from the plan, all deliberate:*
- =session-pause=/=session-resume= *keep* their =emacsclient= call and
  append to the queue alongside it, rather than dropping it as the plan
  said. Dropping it now would stop pausing the live clock at turn
  boundaries while the MCP tools still open real clocks — every interval
  would silently absorb idle time, with nothing yet able to apply the
  queued events instead. Additive now, removed in =feba67eb='s cutover.
- =--aggregate-guideposts= does *not* reuse
  =--merge-time-intervals= as the plan proposed: that merges only
  touching or overlapping intervals and has no gap tolerance, which is a
  different question from clustering nearby points.
- =--parse-iso8601= shape-checks its input before calling
  =date-to-time=. A test caught that =date-to-time= accepts ="nonsense"=
  and returns a near-epoch time, which would sort a garbage line *ahead*
  of every real event — corrupting ordering rather than being skipped.
- Added a generic =--atomic-write= rather than reusing
  =--write-clock-status=, which is hardwired to its own defcustom path.
  Folding the older function onto the new helper is a tidy-up left for
  whoever next touches the statusline.

*Amended 2026-08-07, after the schema was checked against
=clock-template.org=.* The original design had no =note= field, so a
drained queue would have produced bare =CLOCK:= lines and unlabelled
=State "X" from "Y"= lines — a strictly poorer =:LOGBOOK:= than the
template's conventions call for, where every non-=CLOCK:= line carries a
short label. A real gap, missed because the schema was designed against
this heading's own text rather than against the template that defines
the target output.

Added: a nullable =note= field, populated from =tool_input.note=, with
an optional =note= argument on =org_clock_in=, =org_clock_out= and
=org_set_todo= (3-10 words). The Elisp wrappers accept and *ignore* it
deliberately — writing it into the live buffer now would produce exactly
the unreviewed record this design exists to avoid, and there is no
correct place for it in a =:LOGBOOK:= until a human confirms the
interval it belongs to. At apply time it becomes the =\\= continuation
on org's native state-change line, via the =org-store-log-note= path
already confirmed in [[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]]
(the note text is the note buffer's contents; empty yields a bare State
line).

Also fixed =clock_out='s null =id=. The tool takes no =id= argument —
the running clock is the only thing that knows which heading is closing
— so its reply now reports the id it *actually* closed
(="Clocked out: \"H\" (id: ID)"=) and =queue-append= recovers it from
there. Authoritative in a way a caller-supplied argument would not be:
it cannot disagree with what happened. The ="Clocked out:"= prefix is
unchanged, which is all =bin/clock-notify= matches on.

Still open, deliberately: *guidepost span labels*. In the template a
label like =- <09:00>--<09:15> clarify backend schema design= belongs to
a *span*, aggregated from many =pause=/=resume= events — and those come
from hooks Claude never invokes, so no call site can supply one. Default
plan is to inherit the enclosing =clock_in='s note, leaving spans
unlabelled where there is none, with transcript-derived labels
([[id:0c8644ff-f7b1-4789-ac1e-babf2e9fbc8e][0c8644ff]]) as the eventual
upgrade. Worth noting that deferral is a scope call, *not* a capability
gap: reading a transcript is plain file I/O, locatable from =session_id=
alone, and needs none of the MCP sampling machinery
=claude-code-ide= lacks.

Verified: =bin/test= 138/138 (4 new), =bin/queue-append-test= 30/30 (10
new), and =config.el= live-reloaded with the new arities and tool schema
confirmed in the running Emacs.


*Plan staleness audited 2026-08-11.* =event-queue-format.md= has drifted
from what shipped in four places:
- *The watermark design was abandoned.* The plan describes
  =<session_id>.applied= as holding "the =ts= of the last applied
  event," with everything at or before it treated as consumed. The
  implementation stores an applied *set*, because a high-water mark
  cannot advance past a skipped item — observed live 2026-08-07, when a
  real apply wrote no watermark at all and would have re-proposed
  everything.
- *It states the ="Error:"= suppression as working.* It does not, for
  the response shape a real failing MCP call produces; see
  [[id:5e2a1c04-7b3f-4d21-9c88-2f6e0a91b7d3][5e2a1c04]]. The plan
  documents an intent the code does not achieve, which is worse than
  documenting nothing.
- *The schema table is missing three fields* added since: =note= (the
  amendment covers it), =agent_type=, and =from=.
- *The "no auto-rounding" rationale points at machinery that no longer
  exists.* Rounding was retired outright in
  [[id:b74e0f19-5a26-4c83-9d70-8e1c5a2f6b04][b74e0f19]]; the conclusion
  still holds but the reason cited for it, and the =config.el:900/991=
  line references, do not.
*** DONE Rework the MCP tool / hook write paths to append-only :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0
:CREATED:  [2026-08-06 Thu 10:47]
:BLOCKER:  ids(720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1)
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 14:53]--[2026-08-11 Tue 16:59] =>  2:06
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 13:47]--[2026-08-11 Tue 13:55] =>  0:08
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-11 Tue 16:41] \\
  cutover verified by the first post-cutover session
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 13:29]--[2026-08-11 Tue 13:46] =>  0:17
- State "NEXT"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-11 Tue 12:26] \\
  deferred to a fresh session, order recorded
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-11 Tue 12:14] \\
  blocker cleared, starting the cutover
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 12:14]--[2026-08-11 Tue 12:26] =>  0:12
- <2026-08-11 Tue 13:47>--<2026-08-11 Tue 13:55>
- <2026-08-11 Tue 14:53>--<2026-08-11 Tue 16:59>
:END:

[[file:~/.claude/plans/review-and-cutover.md][Plan]]

Planned 2026-08-07 jointly with
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]] as one two-phase
plan (this heading is Phase B); see that heading for why they share one
plan file. The =:BLOCKER:= is not bookkeeping: the moment these tools
stop mutating live state, something has to apply the queued events, and
that something is =720b2dcf=. Landing this first would leave every
transition and interval queued with no way to reach an org file.

*Settled during planning:* the tool-side guards are *retired* rather
than made queue-aware — =bin/hooks/pretooluse-transition-guard= and
=bin/clock-notify= (with =bin/clock-notify-test=) both exist to police
live state against concurrent writers, and after the cutover there are
none. Human review becomes the validation step, which is the point of
the refactor. The org-side hooks (=org-blocker-hook=/=org-trigger-hook=)
stay: they still guard genuine hand-edits made directly in Emacs.

=org_set_todo=, =org_clock_in=, =org_clock_out= become queue-appends
instead of live buffer mutations (per the routing table above).
=bin/hooks/session-pause=/=session-resume= become bare timestamp
appends with no =emacsclient= call at all — this alone is what
retires the =337f7fb2=/=582cc7f4=/=d150c02e= ownership-variable
infrastructure, so double-check nothing else still depends on
=claude-code-ide-org--clock-owner-session-id=/
=--planning-owner-session-id= before deleting them.

Three existing =PostToolUse=/=PreToolUse= hooks don't just port over
unchanged and are easy to overlook since they aren't in the routing
table above (that table is about MCP tools, not the hook layer
sitting alongside them):
- =bin/hooks/pretooluse-transition-guard= validates a transition
  against the heading's *current* state before allowing it — but
  "current" on disk goes stale the moment anything for that heading
  is queued-but-unapplied. Needs to become queue-aware (validate
  against the latest *queued* state for that heading, not disk
  state) or it will wrongly allow/reject transitions once a queue
  exists.
- =bin/clock-notify= exists to cross-check a tool's claimed clock
  result against live =(org-clocking-p)=. Once =org_clock_in=/
  =org_clock_out= never touch live clock state at all, that
  comparison is meaningless — retire it or repurpose it into
  something like "you have N unreviewed updates" instead of "clock
  state mismatch."
- Three clock settings in the user's *personal Doom config*
  (=~/.config/doom/config.el=), added 2026-08-06 — outside this repo,
  which is exactly why they're easy to miss when sweeping the repo for
  clock callers. All three become dead code, because after this
  refactor a clock is only ever live for the microseconds between the
  paired =org-clock-in=/=org-clock-out= calls inside the apply command:
  =claude-code-ide-org--clock-out-if-clocking= on =kill-emacs-hook= and
  on =suspend-hook= (config.el:88-91) exist to catch a clock left
  running across a quit or suspend — =(org-clocking-p)= will
  essentially never be true at those moments any more; and
  =org-clock-out-when-done t= (config.el:67), which fires on =org-todo=
  → =DONE=, now only ever runs inside apply where the clock is already
  closed. Removing them is optional (they're harmless no-ops), but
  leaving them in place misleads the next reader into thinking a
  background clock still exists.
- =bin/hooks/exitplanmode-promote-planning= auto-promotes =PLANNING=
  → =DOING= by reusing the same live clock interval. Under this
  design that becomes just another queued =todo= event stacked on
  the still-unapplied =PLANNING= one — fine, but it means the
  apply-time reconciliation (see the review-and-apply command below)
  has to correctly collapse a =TODO→PLANNING→DOING= event sequence
  into one coherent interval, not replay each event as if
  independent. Concrete supporting case for why the backdated-
  timestamp sub-task below matters as much as it does.


*Plan staleness audited 2026-08-11.* Phase B of =review-and-cutover.md=
inherits the stale watermark and rounding passages noted on
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]], and has a
structural weakness of its own: it is a *list of edits*, not a sequenced
plan. Phase A gets ordering, hook interactions and failure modes; Phase B
gets bullet points. For the epic's single behavior-changing commit — the
one whose rollback story is "git revert" — that asymmetry is worth
correcting before execution rather than during it.

Specifically unaddressed by the bullets: what order the edits land in so
the suite stays green between them, and what the first session after the
cutover is expected to look like, which is the only real check that the
write paths are all actually severed.

*Working order, derived 2026-08-11 before the first edit.* Not a
re-plan: =review-and-cutover.md='s Phase B decisions are settled and
still hold. What it lacked was an order, which is what follows. One of
its bullets is sequenced wrong, and that is the interesting part.

*The guards must be retired WITH the wrappers, not after.* The plan lists
them as separate bullets, which reads as "whenever." Both become
actively harmful the moment the tools stop mutating:
- =pretooluse-transition-guard= validates a requested transition against
  the heading's *current disk state*. Once a transition only queues, disk
  stops moving — so after queueing =TODO -> DOING=, the guard still sees
  =TODO= and would *reject* the following =DOING -> DONE= as illegal. It
  does not merely go stale; it starts blocking correct work.
- =clock-notify= cross-checks a tool's claimed clock result against live
  =(org-clocking-p)=. Once =org_clock_in= never opens a clock, that
  comparison reports a mismatch on *every* call.

*And session-pause/resume must come after the wrappers, not before.* If
they drop their =emacsclient= call while the tools still open real
clocks, nothing pauses at a turn boundary and every interval inflates
with idle time — the exact reason the additive step was taken during
[[id:32272061-1d78-4726-b13b-90338edb2ba5][32272061]] instead of the
plan's original one-shot.

So:

1. *Wrappers + guards, together.* =org_set_todo=/=org_clock_in=/
   =org_clock_out= validate the =:ID:= and return "queued, pending
   review" without touching a buffer; delete
   =bin/hooks/pretooluse-transition-guard=, =bin/clock-notify= and
   =bin/clock-notify-test= with their =.claude/settings.json= entries.
   Leaves clocking inert, which is correct — apply is the writer now.
2. *=session-pause=/=session-resume=* drop =emacsclient= and become bare
   =queue-append= wrappers.
3. *=exitplanmode-promote-planning=* queues a =todo= event instead of
   driving elisp. Sequenced here because it is the case that most needs
   apply to collapse a =TODO -> PLANNING -> DOING= run into one interval
   rather than replaying three.
4. *Ownership variables* and their machinery
   (=--clock-owner-session-id=, =--planning-owner-session-id=,
   =--maybe-record-planning-owner=, =--promote-planning-to-doing=). Last
   because steps 2 and 3 are what remove their readers; deleting earlier
   would break code still calling them.

Tests move with each step rather than after: their assertions invert from
"the buffer and clock changed" to "an event was appended and the buffer
is untouched," and a suite left asserting the old contract in between
would be noise rather than a signal.

Still one commit, per the plan — the order above is how the edits are
made and the suite is run, not a sequence of commits. =git revert= as
rollback only works if the cutover is atomic.

*Executed 2026-08-11, in the recorded order, one working tree.*

1. *Wrappers + guards.* =org_clock_in= validates the =:ID:= and returns
   ="Queued clock_in on \"H\"; pending review."=; =org_clock_out= returns
   a constant and takes no =:ID:= at all; =org_set_todo= validates the
   =:ID:= *and* the keyword against =org-todo-keywords-1= (buffer-local,
   so it checks what the file declares rather than a list hard-coded in
   the wrapper) and returns ="Queued todo -> DOING (was NEXT): ...="=.
   Deleted =bin/hooks/pretooluse-transition-guard=, =bin/clock-notify=,
   =bin/clock-notify-test= and their =settings.json= entries.
2. *=session-pause=/=session-resume=* are now one =exec= into
   =queue-append= each, with no =emacsclient= call.
3. *=exitplanmode-promote-planning=* reads the session's own queue file
   for the heading it most recently queued =PLANNING= on and appends a
   =DOING= event beside it.
4. *Ownership variables* (=--log-session-id=, =--clock-owner-session-id=,
   =--planning-owner-session-id=) and their machinery
   (=--maybe-record-planning-owner=, =--promote-planning-to-doing=,
   =bin/hooks/posttooluse-record-planning-owner=) deleted.

*Three things went beyond the recorded order, each because leaving them
would have left something that lies:*
- *The one-minute clock floor* (=--minimum-clock-seconds=,
  =--clock-out-floor-time=) went with =clock_out='s body. It was a
  write-path policy and apply is the write path now — and apply
  deliberately writes exact confirmed endpoints, so a floor would be the
  rounding this epic already retired once.
- *The three MCP tool descriptions* — model-facing text still promising
  "Opens a CLOCK entry and starts the Emacs clock timer." Every one now
  says the event is queued and the file does not change, because the
  description is the only thing telling a session not to expect a later
  read to reflect its own call.
- *=queue-append= grew explicit =QUEUE_APPEND_ID/STATE/FROM= overrides*,
  for events with no tool call behind them. The alternative — having
  =exitplanmode= synthesise a fake =tool_response= for the existing sed
  to recover — was rejected as unreadable.

*=clock_out= no longer reports an =:ID:=, and does not need to.* It used
to recover the running clock's heading so =queue-append= could sed it
out of the reply. =--queue-events-by-id= already attributes a null-id
=clock_out= to whichever heading that session's last =clock_in= named,
which is strictly better: the only clock that can be running now belongs
to the *human*, so reading one would attribute a session's interval to
whatever the user happens to be clocking.

*Verified:* =bin/test= 145/145, =bin/queue-append-test= green (12 new
checks, including that an override beats =tool_input= and that both
post-cutover reply shapes still parse), =bin/exitplanmode-promote-test=
green — a *new* harness, 15 checks. That last one is worth naming: the
ExitPlanMode hook had no automated coverage before and could not
reasonably have had any, being a here-doc of elisp handed to
=emacsclient=; it is now a bash read of one queue file, and the
cross-session guard that used to need an in-memory ownership variable is
tested as what it now is — structural, since the file is per-session.

*The test suite inverted rather than shrank.* 28 tests failed on the
first run after the wrappers changed, every one of them a test that used
a wrapper as *setup* ("clock in", "make it DOING") rather than testing
its contract. Two helpers —
=claude-code-ide-org-test--clock-in-for-real= and =--set-todo-for-real=
— now say so explicitly, which makes the remaining wrapper tests
unambiguous: they assert nothing happened. Net 168 → 145 ERT tests: 16
deleted with the code they covered (session pause/resume ownership,
PLANNING promotion, the clock floor, the clock_out id), the rest
rewritten in place.

*Live-verified against the real =TODO.org=, not just batch.* Reloaded
=config.el= and unbound the ten deleted symbols by hand (=load-file=
evaluates what is in a file; it does not unbind what was removed from
it). Then called all three wrappers on this very heading: each returned
its "pending review" string, =set_todo "WAIT"= reported =(was DOING)=
while the heading *stayed* =DOING=, an undeclared keyword and a bad
=:ID:= both came back =Error:=-prefixed — the prefix =queue-append= drops
on — and the file's sha256 was byte-identical before and after with the
buffer unmodified.

*One piece of pre-cutover state was cleaned up deliberately:* the clock
this session opened on this heading at 13:29, before the wrappers
stopped clocking. Nothing would ever have closed it — the =Stop= hook is
queue-only as of this commit — so it was closed through =org-clock-out=,
which is exactly what the old hook would have done at that moment.
Left open it would have tripped the stale-interval recovery check
tomorrow and blocked this heading from reaching =DONE=.

*Not claimed, and the reason this heading is not DONE:* the first
post-cutover session. Everything above is batch, shell, and
=emacsclient=; the plan's own gate for this work is "what the first
session after the cutover looks like," and that cannot happen inside the
session that made the change — =.claude/settings.json= is read once at
session start, so *this* session is still running the old hook wiring,
pointing at three scripts that no longer exist. The first real check is
a fresh session: its =SessionStart=/=UserPromptSubmit= hooks fire the new
scripts, its tool calls queue instead of mutating, and a review pass
applies them.

*Gate met 2026-08-11. Closing.* The next session began 95 seconds after
the cutover commit (=220ba78= 13:50:24, =SessionStart= 13:51:59) and
exercised all three clauses:

- *Hooks fired the new scripts.* Every =UserPromptSubmit= and =Stop= in
  that session produced exactly one =resume=/=pause= in the queue, with
  no spurious or missing entries, matched one-for-one against the
  transcript's own prompt and =stop_hook_summary= records.
- *Tool calls queued instead of mutating.* =org_clock_in=,
  =org_clock_out= and =org_set_todo= were each called against live
  headings; TODO.org's sha256 was unchanged across all of them.
- *A review pass applied them.* Two state transitions were confirmed and
  written with native =State= lines, the watermark advanced by exactly
  those two timestamps, and the =.jsonl= was left intact.

*What the gate caught, which is the point of having had one.* The
session found a live escape of
[[id:5e2a1c04-7b3f-4d21-9c88-2f6e0a91b7d3][5e2a1c04]] — a =clock_in= for
a nonexistent =:ID:= entering the queue, because the =Error:= guard
could not see a content-block =tool_response=. Fixed and closed. Its
downstream damage was then observed for real at the review pass: the
phantom captured a guidepost interval, the heading actually being worked
on recorded nothing, and the 205 seconds proposed were idle time. None
of that was reachable from batch.

*No =clock_out= was queued when closing this heading, deliberately.* The
transition rule that =DOING -> DONE= closes the clock was written for the
live-clocking model. There is no live clock to close; the =clock_in=
queued against this heading at 15:10:36 is orphaned — it produced no
review item at all — and a =clock_out= appended now would be captured by
the same =dead-beef= cursor, manufacturing a fourth permanently
un-appliable queue item. Recorded rather than worked around, since
"what closing a heading means when the clock is a queued concept" is a
real gap in the transition rules that
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]] should settle.
