  drawer and machinery fully retired
- State "DOING"      from "MAYBE"      [2026-08-11 Tue 11:33] \\
  user decided: delete SESSIONS entirely
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 11:33]--[2026-08-11 Tue 11:38] =>  0:05
:END:

Captured 2026-08-07 from conversation, prompted by asking a narrow
question — is =claude-code-ide-org-clock-out= still useful once this is
over? — that turned out to have a much wider answer. Deliberately
*deferred*: this is codebase simplification, best done once the dust has
settled and it is clear what can actually be jettisoned, not decided
speculatively now. Hence =MAYBE= and the =:BLOCKER:= on
[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]].

*What is potentially dead, with call counts as of 2026-08-07:*
- =claude-code-ide-org-clock-out= — exactly *one* caller
  (=claude-code-ide-org-session-pause=, config.el:471), which
  =feba67eb= removes. Nothing else in the module, the hooks, or the
  user's Doom config calls it; the Doom hooks use raw =org-clock-out=.
- =claude-code-ide-org-consolidate-history= — two callers, both likely
  to go: inside the wrapper above (config.el:438), and inside
  =close-open-interval= (config.el:901), which belongs to the recovery
  feature [[id:7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c][7771fc63]]
  questions the premise of. Not an MCP tool, so no external caller.
- With it, the =:SESSIONS:= text machinery it exists to drive:
  =--parse-session-lines=, =--format-session-line=,
  =--consolidate-sessions-text= (config.el:1045-1078).
- And plausibly the =:SESSIONS:= *drawer itself*.

*The case that the drawer is now redundant:* CLAUDE.md describes it as
"the bracketing history: a plain timestamped log of every pause and
resume, so the full wall-clock arc of the task — including the gaps —
stays visible." Under the new model that is exactly what the guidepost
annotations in =:LOGBOOK:= do, derived from the same =pause=/=resume=
events. Supporting, though neither is decisive: =clock-template.org=,
the worked example defining the target output, contains *zero* mentions
of =:SESSIONS:= — but its own legend scopes it to ":LOGBOOK: conventions",
so that silence may be scope rather than deletion. And after the cutover
the only remaining writer would be =org_log_background_plan=, appending
a single ="Background-planned"= entry — thin justification for a whole
drawer plus parser plus consolidator.

*The case against, which deserves real weight:* CLAUDE.md presents the
two mechanisms as deliberately and co-equally separate, not as an
accident to be tidied away, and that separation was a considered
decision rather than drift. Retiring it is a reversal, so it should be
argued on its merits rather than justified by "nothing calls it any
more."

*Do not lose these three things* — they live inside the wrapper today
and are still needed by
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]'s apply step:
=org-clock-out-remove-zero-time-clocks= bound nil (or a short confirmed
interval is dropped at the org level before it can be written); the
buffer save; and =--log-source= attribution. That last one is the
easiest to lose silently — =--log-event= defaults to ="hand-edit"= when
nothing binds it, so every audit record written by apply would be
misattributed. That attribution matters *more* after the cutover, not
less, since apply becomes the only writer of live state.

*Also to decide, not just "delete or keep":* what happens to the
=:SESSIONS:= data already sitting in TODO.org and DONE.org headings.
Retiring the mechanism does not retroactively delete history — the
options are leaving it in place as inert historical record, converting
it to =:LOGBOOK:= guidepost annotations, or dropping it. Probably the
first, but it is a separate question from whether anything new gets
written.


*Contradiction with the epic's plan, found 2026-08-11.* This heading
records the =:SESSIONS:= question as deliberately deferred and =MAYBE=.
=plan-this-task-clever-cupcake.md='s decision 5 records the opposite —
retirement *confirmed with the user* on 2026-08-06, with the further
consequence that =2b0988ed= becomes moot and should be CANCELLED.

One of the two is wrong, and it is not a bookkeeping detail: it decides
whether a drawer, its parser, its consolidator and their tests get
deleted. This heading is the later document and was written after a day
of implementation, which argues for it — but the plan claims explicit
user confirmation, which is stronger evidence than recency. Resolve it
before either is acted on.

*Reframed 2026-08-11, and then answered.* The arguments recorded above —
=org_log_background_plan='s lone remaining write, and CLAUDE.md's
presentation of the two drawers as co-equal — are the weak ones. The
question that actually decides this heading is: *what is a drained queue
file?*

After the cutover, =:LOGBOOK:= holds only the human-confirmed residue —
the intervals someone approved at review. Every pause and resume the
human declined to turn into an interval exists *only* in the raw queue
files. But CLAUDE.md's stated job for =:SESSIONS:= is the full wall-clock
arc *including the gaps*. So:
- if queue files are disposable, the arc-including-gaps has no durable
  home, and something has to keep it;
- if queue files are permanent, the =.jsonl= does that job strictly
  better than the drawer ever did, carrying =session_id=, =agent_id=,
  =agent_type= and =source= — none of which =:SESSIONS:= records.

The two plans disagreed on exactly this: the epic plan contemplated
deleting fully-applied files, while =event-queue-format.md= argued
deletion "loses history the review UI may still want."

*The user's answer, 2026-08-11:* neither — archive drained files rather
than deleting them, and make ingestion idempotent so an archived file
can be restored and reprocessed after a logic change or a corruption.
That makes queue files permanent by default without committing to never
tidying the live directory, and it is tracked as
[[id:78f485a8-e808-43ce-af5f-277a5c97c471][78f485a8]].

*Consequence for this heading: retire the drawer.* With the raw history
durable and restorable, =:SESSIONS:= has no job left that the queue does
not do better. What remains is the narrower question this heading
already poses correctly — what happens to the =:SESSIONS:= data already
sitting in TODO.org and DONE.org — where leaving it in place as inert
legacy history is still the obvious answer.

*Decided and half executed 2026-08-11.* The user's call, and it went
further than either option this heading had contemplated: delete the
existing drawers *entirely* rather than leaving them as inert legacy
history, on the grounds that they are distracting, that their retrospective
information content is low, and that post-cutover history will be much more
informative. The =:BLOCKER:= on
[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]] is therefore
discharged by decision rather than by the cutover landing.

*The data was worth measuring before deleting it.* 49 drawers, 105 entry
lines across TODO.org and DONE.org. 91 were exactly the day-collapsed
=Paused=/=Resumed= noise described — already lossy, since consolidation
had long since reduced each day to one min-to-max pair. The other 14 were
a different kind of record and were surfaced explicitly before anything
was removed: 9 =Background-planned [ts] (session ID)= entries, the only
trace of which session background-planned a heading and when, and 5
=Paused ... (recovered)= markers from crash repair. The user chose to
delete those too — "entirely means entirely" — knowing what they were.
Everything is recoverable from git: both files were committed and pushed
before the deletion.

*Done:*
- All three =--log-session-event= call sites removed (clock-in, clock-out,
  session-resume), and the function itself deleted. Nothing writes a
  =:SESSIONS:= line by any path.
- =org_log_background_plan= no longer records its =session-id=. The
  parameter stays in the signature, so the MCP schema and callers are
  unaffected and there is an obvious place to reattach attribution as a
  queued event.
- All 49 drawers deleted, via =org-element-map= over real =drawer=
  elements rather than a text regex, so a =":SESSIONS:"= mentioned in
  prose was never at risk. Verified: zero drawers, zero orphaned entry
  lines, both files still parse, heading counts unchanged (69 and 23).
- Tests: two whose entire subject was the drawer deleted; three rewritten
  to assert what the wrapper actually promises (the clock closes, the
  clock lands back on the right heading) plus the drawer's *absence*, so
  its return would be a failure rather than a silent regression.
- CLAUDE.md and the org skill updated. The "cosmetic quirk" about
  =:SESSIONS:=/=:LOGBOOK:= ordering is resolved outright: with one drawer
  there is nothing to order.

*Remaining, and the reason this heading is still DOING:* the machinery is
now dead code that parses a format nothing writes —
=--parse-session-lines=, =--format-session-line=,
=--consolidate-sessions-text=, =consolidate-history='s =:SESSIONS:= half,
the recovery scan for an unclosed =Resumed=, and =close-open-interval='s
=:SESSIONS:= branch, plus their tests. It is inert rather than harmful,
which is why stopping here is a coherent state, but leaving it misleads
the next reader exactly as CLAUDE.md warns.

*Worth knowing before that cleanup starts:* =consolidate-history= may have
nothing left to do at all. [[id:b74e0f19-5a26-4c83-9d70-8e1c5a2f6b04][b74e0f19]]
removed its rounding and merging, leaving the =:LOGBOOK:= half a
normaliser that returns its input; removing the =:SESSIONS:= half removes
the only transformation it still performs. That makes it a candidate for
deletion outright rather than trimming — which is what this heading
predicted, and which reaches into
[[id:7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c][7771fc63]]'s territory, since
=close-open-interval= is one of its two callers.

*Machinery removed 2026-08-11; this heading is now fully executed.*
=--parse-session-lines=, =--format-session-line= and
=--consolidate-sessions-text= are deleted. =consolidate-history= lost its
=:SESSIONS:= half, =close-open-interval= lost both its
"Paused ... (recovered)" write *and* its =consolidate-history= call, and
the stale-interval scan no longer looks for an unclosed =Resumed= —
=--entry-open-interval= and =find-stale-open-intervals= dropped
=:sessions-open= throughout. Audited afterwards: not one ="SESSIONS"=
string literal remains anywhere in =config.el=; every surviving mention is
prose explaining the retirement.

*Two consequential decisions taken inside this cleanup, both arguable:*
- *=consolidate-history= was kept, not deleted,* though it is now close
  to a no-op — [[id:b74e0f19-5a26-4c83-9d70-8e1c5a2f6b04][b74e0f19]] took
  its rounding and merging, and this took its second drawer, leaving only
  ordering. Kept because
  [[id:af7d3687-6d1e-4480-a9e2-df772785713f][af7d3687]] is precisely a
  change to this function; deleting it now would mean recreating it. It
  has no automatic caller, which is the point.
- *=close-open-interval= no longer consolidates.* Rewriting a whole
  drawer as a side effect of repairing one line is the shape behind both
  [[id:ba8249c1-28cd-4ff1-918b-4b8439345d9a][ba8249c1]] and =b74e0f19=,
  and consolidation had nothing left to contribute there. This reaches
  into [[id:7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c][7771fc63]]'s territory,
  since =close-open-interval= was =consolidate-history='s only remaining
  caller — that subsystem's fate is now a cleaner question than it was.

*Verified:* =bin/test= 163/163 (three tests deleted as their whole
subject was the drawer, four rewritten to assert the drawer's *absence*
so its return fails rather than passing silently). Live-reloaded, with
the three deleted functions explicitly =fmakunbound= — a =load-file=
re-evaluates what is in a file but does not unbind what was removed from
it, so the running image would otherwise have kept them.

*One incident worth recording, because it proves the point.* Immediately
after deleting all 49 drawers I clocked out, and a drawer came back — on
*this heading*. The live Emacs was still running the pre-edit writer,
because =config.el= had been changed on disk but never reloaded. Caught
by the post-deletion count, not by foresight; the reload precondition
should have been stated before the check rather than after it.
